## 212. 2026-07-11
<a id="t-2075971293116092700"></a>

*Single post · [2075971293116092700](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2075971293116092700)*

from Napoleon's 1812 retreat from Russia...crazy detail on the human condition

a lot of bios don't go into the Vilnius disaster

after the Berezina, the Grande Armée marched 150 miles in temperatures of minus 35...men just toppling over dead, mid-march, from the extreme cold

Vilnius was supposed to be salvation, a massive supply hub packed with food, shelter, ammo, etc

The city was overwhelmed on arrival but the soldiers mostly were able to get a good meal and a warm night's rest

This is where Napoleon decides to return to Paris to stem rumors of a coup, and having realized the campaign was lost, he abandons the army and puts Murat in charge.

Murat almost immediately loses his nerve, freaks out when some Russian cossacks appear, and orders a premature evacuation of Vilnius the next morning

This is the material on the page...the men were broken, they couldn't move:

--

"When he heard the bugle sound the alarm in the early hours and began rousing his men, many, even veterans of the Egyptian and Italian campaigns, simply refused to budge. “That night of complete rest around a good fire had been enough to extinguish their courage and their energy,” he wrote. “They were overcome by a general drowsiness, a heaviness in the head which seemed to obscure the faculty of thought. Stupefied, and as if drunk, they attempted to get to their feet, only to fall back heavily.” The story was the same in other units.

--

then, Prince Wilhelm describes stopping at Vilnius as an error, as the men would have been better off continuing to march:

--

“Instead of staying a whole day in Vilna, it would have been far better to continue the retreat without stopping,” wrote Prince Wilhelm of Baden. “Many officers, calling on their last reserves of strength, would have reached the German frontier and would have been saved.” 

When it was time to leave he tried to persuade them to come with him, but after one night of release, these men who had come so far found they had the strength to go no further. “We had for some time been measuring out our forces in order to reach this city in which we believed we would find what we needed to satisfy our most imperious needs; rest, bread, and Vilna had come to form a trinity which was united in our minds as a single hope, and as a result we were clear in our minds that we would go no further than this city,” wrote Adrian de Mailly.

--

disaster ensues, some 10k-20k troops flee with Murat and Ney

they leave 20-30k behind in the city who were unable to move

The Russians arrive to what is basically "a frozen morgue"

most of the men left behind die from typhus, starvation, and hypothermia

the rest are killed or captured by Cossacks, with the locals joining in so they don't get attacked next

tens of thousands of French troops get buried in massive trenches, bodies which are still occasionally uncovered by construction workers today.

but the psychological breaking point is the part that is most fascinating to me, that stopping for rest was their downfall, and that they would have been better off continuing to march in the freezing cold on no rest

tells u something fascinating about the human spirit, and the ability to summon courage when most needed...

> QT @gregoryblotnick: for fans of Roberts’ “Napoleon: A Life” bio, this is a must https://t.co/s2pEXs34Q1

## 214. 2026-07-11
<a id="t-2076053183575781653"></a>

*Single post · [2076053183575781653](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2076053183575781653)*

on risk, urgency, fear, failure, embracing pain and uncertainty, work/life/comp $, taking the long view, and getting the most out of life

lots of answers + misc stuff cobbled from DM's, all tied to a very common question:

"how do I know when it's time?"

1/9

I find myself using this image a lot as a mental template, even if not exactly as written (Maslow)

most are familiar with it, u have the four levels of basic "stuff" like food and shelter and then some measure of respect/belonging and self-esteem

if u have all those, u are at the highest level -- u are in a self-actualized state

every day u wake up in this state, is a perfect day for u to get busy and make shit happen

because it doesn't get any higher...u are 100% all-systems go, ur visibility is as good as it will get.

2.

and so I would say esp if you're somewhere age 25-40, and have some nagging urge or tug, like

-"I want to do xyz but don't know if it's time"
-"I want to do x but it's risky"
-"I want to quit W2 and start a business"
-"I don't like my current life, I want to go live this other life, but doing so would involve risk, pain, discomfort, uncertainty"

these are all extremely common, everyone u will ever meet has felt some variation of one of those

and I shouldn't say 25-40, there are plenty of guys 40-60 who ponder this shit all day too...like, am I stuck in some fucking jerkoff ass job when I should be doing something else, because I know that my ceiling is so much higher?

thats why the intro Q -- "how do I know when its time?" -- was left intentionally vague.

time for what?

always time for something.

but at a younger age, u don't understand the rhythm of life yet, the boundaries and dimensions and guardrails

I am here to tell u that it does not get any better than where u are right now.

like, nothing more could align in your favor, God is not going to flip a switch for you

3.

if u were to ask "what more could I want to align, what am I missing?"

u will realize "there is absolutely nothing more I could need."

if ur healthy, relatively happy, and ur family and friends are happy and healthy as well, and u have no major pressing issues to deal with...

at the minimum, that qualifies as "a great day."

at the maximum, u should absolutely be launching yourself, like, immediately, at whatever u have nagging u

because its never gonna get any better than where u are, thats the thing.

I think its tempting to hold out some strange vague hope for...something? you don't even know what, but you're hoping, just, something?

never happens brotha, this is just procrastination or timidity or fear in ur pilot seat.

4.

so I'm not going to shove u off a cliff or write some dork ass post like "u should totally go do the thing bro, do the thing now, do hard things"

its more, gently calibrating ur frame of reference and mindset...kind of a "YOU ARE HERE" sign.

if you blast off, it will be from ur current position and lifestyle.

if u imagine urself in a helicopter, about to skydive, it's a matter of u just rocking forward and falling out of the plane.

that's where u are right now, that's the friction, tension, barrier that ur up against...100% mental.

theres no resistance beyond that

if tomorrow, u told everyone u know "hey I'm moving to Japan," you'd get a bunch of random comments and pushback

24 hours later, they've all forgotten and u wont hear about it again. 

no one cares. everyone has their own shit to worry about

u could already be in Japan 24 hours later, they don't know and don't care

5.

ur family, ur friends, they'll weigh in because they're obliged to, but its ur life and ur say is the final say

I say this because people freeze up worrying about what other ppl will think

its 24 hours and then its history

applies for literally everything...u can kill a guy and u will be front-page news, there will be 24 hours where u exist.

and then tomorrow...u go right back to being invisible to the entire world.

dont worry so much about what people think, and remember that ur execution shapes what they think

if u launch a billion-dollar Japanese business, everyone u know: "oh yeah I knew he'd do it...supported him, told him to go, always believed in him" et cetera

if u totally fuck it up: "oh yeah I told him it was a risky idea and not to go, I knew it was a bad move, tried to warn him"

people don't care

also, do ur own due diligence amongst people u know, who randomly moved across the world, took some crazy risk, etc...and ask them, "do you regret the decision you made?"

in my experience it is HEAVILY skewed towards "hell fucking no I don't regret it," even when it didn't pan out. because a lot of times, it shook off chains and cobwebs, and u re-centered how u think about getting the most out of life...things that u would just never ponder when u are in "white collar office" mode.

in both life and markets, u learn only thru action, thru paying tuition

should I trade software, go do 100 software trades and then review your P&L

same idea for most of life's "should I do X" questions

6.

now...the word "urgency" is tricky, but relevant

because time is not on your side

each year, the odds grow that u may end up with a massive fixed cost burden (mortgage/family/kids) which slams the "risk window" shut

u can still do it, people do it all the time, but "25-30 and single with no fixed costs" vs "40 with $200k in annual burn + humans relying on me"...definitely changes everything.

if its just you, your downside is extremely limited

worst-case scenario is something like, broke and unemployed and sleeping on a couch or in a shitty studio.

anyone older will tell u, "that really aint shit" lol

7.

u can cut ur costs to the bone, to just rent + food

somewhere between $3000 and $4000/mo, u can live a good life...a really nice studio is $2500 in south florida which means $2k or less in a lot of cities

so u need to make $40-50k per year to survive

anyone with half a brain can get there in 6-12 months, and after that, u will never work for anyone again. zero reason to, ur self-sufficient.

cutting ur expenses is everything, u can be living a $200k manhattan lifestyle one day, and the next, a $50k one where u live a mile or two from the beach

and u will realize "wait, why am I so much happier with every single day of my life even though my comp just went down 75%"

bc all the best things in life are free. sun, ocean, friends, books, writing, weights...keep shit super simple, minimum possessions, tight circle, and u are not only chilling, but u are chilling for life. once u have ur habits and ur friends locked in, u can ride those til u die.

8.

the digital nomad guys in their 20's and early 30's who make "internet money" and go live in new cities for 6-12 months on a stretch, bopping around LatAm and Europe, they've got it figured it out

that's exactly what u should be doing with ur life at that age, traveling, exploring, becoming financially self-sufficient where u can work from anywhere.

if I was 25-30 and lost/aimless I would just pick one of those guys who seems to have it figured out, offer to work for him for cheap, move somewhere cheap like LatAm or Vietnam, spend 2 years learning his playbook, and then go off on ur own.

"can I do that?"

dog its ur life, u can do whatever the fuck u want.  if u leave some $125k white-collarish office job, and within 2-3 years u can generate $80-100k on ur own

ur there, ur at parity, and all those mf's stuck in the office will HATE you for pulling it off.

but this is me talking, as if I was 25. idk if this is right for everyone

although I just can't see anyone saying "yeah I'd take the $125k city consulting job over the $90k of solopreneur fully-remote internet money"

a man, a laptop, LLM's and starlink. u can build fucking empires with that and u can do it from anywhere.

9.

the world changed so fucking much from 2020 to say 2022, and then accelerated again with AI.

u have to recognize, no one has a clue whats going on right now, this shit is wide open for anybody.

"you have to go work at these 4 firms and do this and that and take the metro north every single day for 40 years"

u dont have to do any of that shit

u have to make enough money to pay ur rent and afford food, and after that, its all blue sky

if you can scale to $150k over 5 years, and ur buddy is working at JP Morgan in NYC making $140k, like, how is anyone going to tell you shit lol

"I work better hours, I work for myself, I work from anywhere, and, I almost forgot, I make more money. What were u saying?"

conversation over

mogged from every possible angle

dont ever let ppl try and put u in some outdated box like "no u have to work at a NYC bank in ur 20's, bc thats what u do"...stuck in 2010 or some shit

the world changed in 2020 and again in 2022, toss the old rules out, go build ur dream life for urself.

END/LONG GAME

if u happen to have high expenses, u could still go get a job if u take risk and fail, or maybe u learned from ur failures and u run it back -- chances are u made connections with either "good ppl to work with/for" or "providers of capital"

if u have good people and access to capital u can just keep putting up shots over and over for the next 50 years, trying to build and create shit and learn from ur failures

if ur wired a certain way, this is an extremely fulfilling life, esp right now...one man and an LLM can launch literally ANYTHING into fucking orbit, anything

and u can easily find another 5-10 like-minded souls on X for you to link with, or build with, or brainstorm big dreams with

this is such a great time in history to be alive for a young scrappy entrepreneurial type who loves building and creating shit. 

there are so many ways to make crazy upside, that I can't see how u stay motivated at some "I'm gonna take my $100k flat comp each year...maybe a $5k discretionary bonus."

the notion of "taking risk" by leaving that setup, rebasing ur expenses to $50k, selling everything, and building a new life + multiple businesses from scratch...that does not sound "risky" to me, at all

once u scale to $50k income, the risk is gone, u made it, ur alive, ur self-sufficient, and u know what?

being able to say "for the next 50 years, I will NEVER get fired, because I work for myself"...is actually far less "risky" than all these finance/tech jobs where people are getting axed left and right.

--

that's all I've got on this topic for now...peep the graphic below, tape it to ur desk

remember, if u wake up, and u are in the top triangle -- self-actualization -- u are in "make shit happen" territory

and if u don't want to make shit happen, I'm fond of saying: "WHEN YOU HAVE THE CAPACITY TO HELP, HELP."

if you're healthy and happy and actualized, go be of service, go figure out how to be of use to someone, go help someone 5-10 years younger than you

some day u may be sick, or ur struggling with a major life issue, and guess what -- u won't be able to help anyone.

u don't have the capacity to help. so u can't help.

so...

"if you have the capacity to help, help."

Godspeed 😎

> QT @gregoryblotnick: was talking this over with a buddy

age as meaningless #, experience, jung's 1st/2nd half of life, "weeks where decades happen," severe life upheavals...pain risk wisdom, windows of opportunity

--

1/7

so the thing w/ his quote (40s are great, 50s u have a crisis), is that the age itself is sort of meaningless. 

one man's 35 is another's 40, or 45, or 50

it's not the age, it's that everything is driven by experience -- pain, suffering, failure

"there are decades when nothing happens, and there are weeks when decades happen" lenin

so... if you were to just lay on the couch for 20 years straight, from 30 to 50, not doing anything, u aren't magically granted some wisdom as the years pass

that's a falsehood to be dispelled bc I've gotten tweets like, "oh at 30 this happens, and 35 this happens," I might've said the same younger, idk

but if u do nothing, here's what happens: nothing

all u will do is decay.

2/7

whereas if u are 31 and u just go through it all in a single year, ur mind goes from 31 to 50 (in the best way possible)...experience is what makes someone seasoned and wise to the ways of the world

this dictates how ur friend group revolves, there are ppl who play it safe lifestyle-wise and there are people who take risk

paths diverge, u were on the same highway younger, now u just each took ur off ramp

jung's "1st half/2nd half of life" framework is prob the best

u will have friends who cross the plane (due to pain, experience), whereas others don't....idk if it's your choice

3/7

all the ppl I'm closest friends with, had their lives fall apart at some point in their late 20's or 30's or early 40's

lost all their $, divorce, drugs, lawsuit, jail, business failure

all the same personality type, high energy, high risk appetite...not the type to ever say "x happened to me," not sympathy seekers. just a part of ur nature + usually paired with, "the speed limit was 65, I should've been doing 75-80, I was at 100"

like u were just going at too fast a speed, for too long, where a bad outcome of some sort was due. 

and thats where u finally come face to face with ur own mortality, and thats when u shed the skin of ur 1st half of life and enter the 2nd half of life.

4/7

life goes on. ur new life is totally different from ur old life, but life goes on

and as time goes on, u see lots of other people, normal, quiet, God-fearing...who go thru periods of severe upheaval

they didn't even do anything wrong, like...u just get used to the fact that ur life, or anyone's life, can change, forever, in an single day. 

and the life u knew, is gone.

but out of the ppl I'm close with, every single person, if asked, "would u go back in time if u could and erase the actions + consequences"... says fuck no.

because u see who u are now, vs who u were then, and its like, are u kidding me, that guy wasn't built to be calm in chaos. he thought he was, but he wasn't.

and if u reflect on any of ur own errors or mistakes u will see its the same answer.  

everything u know about markets came from losing $...u cant go back and get the $, without giving up the experience/wisdom.

plus...the way I phrase it is that "fears melt away as they are faced head on."

nothing in life is ever as good as it seems, and nothing is as bad as it seems...pain is intense and short-lasting, and then u recognize the fear was always greater than reality.

and at a certain point u have made every mistake in the book, which has a massive positive impact on ur life

would u advise that someone go thru the consequences to get there...not intentionally. but dont live in fear either

and in this light, u recognize regret as silly and pointless in the self-pity/self-flagellation sense. just do better.

5/7

so that gets to the mcconaughey quote below which doesn't quite track

he says "40's were great but I see my 50's midlife crisis coming"

my buddy was like, "his 50-something, was my 30-something."

and that's what I said in the intro, and I agree

the comments on the 40s are dead on, but they usually come from someone who was just shipwrecked where u change ur life.

and hes setting expectations poorly for the majority of ppl who will inevitably have something turn their life upside down in their 40's, despite dodging the reaper so far.

6/7

once u understand that time ticks in linear fashion, whereas experience/wisdom come in massive chunks -- "weeks where decades happen" -- the way I see it, is that I want my experience to stay 10-30 years ahead of my age

my biggest concern is that I'm not dying enough deaths

you watch a year go by and compare the time elapsed vs the experience gain

and what would be a good year for most ppl, like, pain and failure sort of morph ur tolerance -- all for the better

ur level of risk, and ability to withstand pain, is now so much higher than normal and higher than what urs used to be. 

u notice it as hardship/adversity just sort of bounces off, and other people freak out over things where ur thinking, "u better pray nothing more major than this happens bc u are ill-equipped"

but each day that u do not die new deaths, or risk dying new deaths, whatever that means to u...is another day where the reaper closes the gap.

and ur age catches up to your experience

I don't have a crystal ball but when ur 80, I get the sense these moments and feelings are what u regret, far moreso than any mistakes u made.

the feeling of wasted time is never pleasant, ever

7/7

is this a good framework or one to mull over - unclear and it's out of my control. 

when ur gut, ur spirit talk to u, u listen.

it goes without saying to think for urself, never blindly take advice from any stranger on the internet or walk their path...shit might be fucked up, but work for them, but its not for u.

everything has to be unique to u, I'm merely here waving mirrors and lights around hoping it helps u see urself clearer.

I would frame mine if it resonates, in the healthiest light, as:

"I know where my ceiling is, I am nowhere near it...I'm not growing fast enough, I can handle a lot more discomfort/pain/risk than I am taking right now, and my lifestyle permits it."

and when all these things align, like, u gotta fuckin shoot.

thats what ppl don't get..."all clear" is the green light. 

there is no starting gun, only a window of opportunity, which remains open for God only knows how long.

its only when the light turns red, window slams shut, and and they're stuck, that they look back: "shit...the opening was there for 2 years, I was just being a pussy."

end

this is the answer that anyone will give u, to every and any question beginning with: "how do I know when its time to (take x risk)"

u never do, no one ever has....age old question, one I used to ask, always gonna be met with a shrug.

"motherfucker just went for it"

and thats 100% bw u and the man in the mirror...there's nothing on twitter for ya.

c'est la guerre ⚔️

## 216. 2026-07-12
<a id="t-2076284392863904012"></a>

*Single post · [2076284392863904012](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2076284392863904012)*

known unknowns, fear, risk, countervailing forces, avoidance of pain, what ppl will think

"how do I know when it's time?" (part 2)

same topic as QT...u have an idea or something u want to do, and u don't know if it's time.

but you are 100% stuck in solo mental warfare.

we are going to nail down as many variables as possible, and make concrete from the intangible

this is how u exit mental warfare mode, or at least create some order from chaos

1. What are the major countervailing forces?

what lurks in the "no" column of your mind?

they tend to be:

- what people will think
- fear
- avoidance of pain

The first one, it can be important, but there are also times where it doesn't matter and you have a false belief system.

If you were to take a risk, random ideas being...build a business, create videos, write a book, do a podcast ....anything where you think, "I risk looking stupid in public."

these are zero downside scenarios when you fail, there is no "what people will think"

you always seem to gain 1000x that in the process.

people aren't thinking about you kid. maybe your content flitters across their feed, maybe they like it, or ignore it, or someone takes 10 seconds to comment "u look like a fgt" and then keeps scrolling

no matter what, you're ancient history within hours

2. 

on starting a business and failing

millions of people have opinions on this

consensus imo: "more upside than downside."

put aside money gained or lost, it matters but it also comes and goes like water, and it ends up being less important for the enjoyment of life than you anticipated.

we covered this in the QT, so we'll keep moving

next is fear

you are afraid of failure. this is natural. but keep in mind everything you've ever read about failure... it's embraced, encouraged, welcomed, and suffering breeds growth.

there's a reason for all this talk, because it's real.

I can't un-fear you, unfortunately, but I can tell you that there is a pretty reliable method for conquering a fear.

you take a deep breath, u grab ur nuts, and u go sprinting at it at full speed. 

duality of life. nothing is ever as good as it seems, but nothing -- including fear -- is ever as bad as it seems.

"fears melt away as they are faced head-on."

3. 

last is avoidance of pain.

This is tricky because to accomplish anything worth accomplishing, you have to leave some possibility open that shit is just going to get fucked up and not work.

You will be in pain, you'll lose relationships, friends, money

everything is risk/reward. this is the risk side of the equation.

I think about this exact topic a lot, because I've gone back and forth over the years between

>100% all-in on one idea
>making smaller bets, planting seeds, buying OTM calls, trying to get as many "small downside + massive upside" situations working for me as possible

I don't know if either is "right," I'm just trying to figure out which is right for me

The former is my default way of operating, "burn the boats" in everything

The latter sounds very intelligent, big-brained, "hard to lose,"

way more prudent than going all-in on urself...diversified, "somethings gotta work," 

blah blah blah...

4.

if I got a stock pitch like that, as a long, I'd say "no fuckin chance it works" 

nothing is that obvious

wow yeah I'd love a portfolio full of "down 0% up 100%'s"

my buddy and I used to call these FLAs🚨

short for "Free Lunch Alert 🚨"

it's down 0%, up 100%? 

guarantee it goes down 75% in a straight line, express, no stops, transfer at jamaica.

but next time someone sends u a garbage pitch u could be real passive aggressive and hit them with the 

"wow FLA🚨"

ADBE at 7x...u don't say

that is a scrumptious free lunch right there u have found, my friend

ty, tyvm...in fact, ty hilton (the summa of ty's)

feels like just last month adbe was 8x, then before that 9x, then 10x

each month a turn of multiple gets lopped off and the free lunch becomes even free'er

what usually happens with these is everything is fine for 4-5 quarters

then they guide EPS 50% below street and the "true" multiple reveals itself not as 8x, but 16x

expensive lunch alert 💰

5.

but on risk/reward, the two scenarios I laid out before -- I sense that God won't let you thread the needle

you can't take no risk, and expect big reward

I'm quietly hoping this happens but deep down I know it's just not the way the world works 

also because putting your ass in the line will motivate you, and that motivation is probably what gets the W in the long run

so, I am not a serial entrepreneur, but my gut says that you can only fuck around for so long with smaller ideas and small dick money and small conviction

it's pretty rare that I know what to do, and choose not to do it

but here I'm once burned, twice shy

I know the correct move is to figure out your best idea and push some fucking chips in

and that it's just mental masturbation, tugging ur worm in the mirror, until u go for it

but u trade markets long enough, u develop scars lol...just a walking encyclopedia of all the L's you've taken.

I'm torn bw "what I know works for me" and "recency bias where it actually didnt work for u"...instinct uber alles.

6.

going back in time...

if I was say 25-35, and trying to map out the future

this is how I would break it down

we talked about the risks of risk, lose $, lose friends, divorce, etc

your comfort with your risk scenario, this downside scenario, is what really dictates everything.

Some people have zero risk appetite, who I have known my whole life...they would just never think this way, they're not wired for it

Others intuitively embrace risk in all things and in all forms.

So if you have been trading markets, now you have risk in the form of "what does my downside in xyz real life scenario look like?" 

it's just a risk.

there's no dollars, but risk is risk.

Is this risk of pain worth the rewards?

Yes, boom, jump off the cliff.

if it works great, if not, I took what felt like a favorable risk/reward proposition.

blackjack mindset....9,999 hands to go.

so there are minds that are constantly viewing everything in risk terms, underwriting risk, and will take pretty much any risk thats heavily skewed in their favor.

what are the risks of writing a book, of sending this tweet, relative to the rewards? favorable, lets go.

8.

If you already think this way, then all near-term opportunities, or any risk/reward proposition, can be framed thru the same lens.

It's never different.

And with each risk you grow incrementally more comfortable with risk.

So I think that's the very best advice I could give in helping someone re-wire their mind for: I am going to take lots of risks in my life but they feel new and different and unusual.

They really aren't. Like, take out a piece of paper, or Excel, and list out all the non-monetary shit you are worried about.

9.

-Money: what's risked, what's lost, what would you be left with

-Family/friends: less of a factor than you think, and often, they are anti-risk because they never want to see you in pain. They will tend to steer you towards "white picket fence, W2, American dream, safe, sound, boring life."

So when you deviate from that path, their reaction doesn't mean a whole lot...

"are you sure," "that sounds risky," "why aren't you doing what everyone else does"

and so on.

They mean well, but just be aware there is a permanent bias and not one that has ever detered someone from following their heart.

And then lay out all the other risks and rewards and variables. 

Just stare at it.  Most of it is hyperventilation or shit you can't control.

My hope is that having it staring back at you from a piece of paper, will solidify it to where u say, "fuck it, I now grasp the known unknowns, let's rip."

10.

It will always come down to:

1. Gameplan
2. Daily execution of gameplan.

So all the "what if this" in a risk scenario, well, you'll get there only if you don't exectute. And if you don't execute, you can't complain.

Is that negative outcome something you'd accept if you can't execute? 

If yes, then go.

You can run this out over 50, 100, 200 ideas, the variables don't change.

END

I would even say taking larger and larger risks is the best way to get comfortable, and what I mean is, if you went on some podcast or something, and you talked for an hour, and it went public...a lot of your fears are going to die.

Because people will talk shit, they'll laugh at you, your respective failures and uglinesses will be mentioned as a human...and then 24 hours later they disappear and move on.

And now you understand risk, reward, failure, and the crowd, so now other risks seem less daunting. But you had to take a different, unrelated risk...and it worked as a mechanism, didn't it?

Behind anything that has ever gotten done, ever, is those same two words.

"fuck it"

No one ever had God come down from the sky and say: "now is the time, here's everything you need."

Everything began with some guy or girl with a loosely-formed plan in their head, who just kind of shrugged 

absolutely no fucking idea if this is a good idea

but

I wanna put the plaza hotel right here

fuck it [plop]

some guy legit said

"I want to start a business called Ollie's Bargain Basement"

followed by "fuck it" followed by 1000 fuckin boxes

no fear

All those Portuguese and Spanish mf's just hopping into boats...let's sail West and we'll see what happens, muchachos.

they came in search of America

and they left with a 36-pack of Dove Mango-Cucumber Exfoliating Face Wash from Ollie's Bargain Basement

24 hours later, the Inquisition began

Oswald Spengler spoke of this

--

u have to always ask: 

"why not me?"

Same question everyone else asked, and they made shit happen.

Final quote on risk, from Larry Hite:

"I have two basic rules about winning in trading as well as in life:
1. If you don't bet, you can't win.
2. If you lose all your chips, you can't bet."

> QT @gregoryblotnick: on risk, urgency, fear, failure, embracing pain and uncertainty, work/life/comp $, taking the long view, and getting the most out of life

lots of answers + misc stuff cobbled from DM's, all tied to a very common question:

"how do I know when it's time?"

1/9

I find myself using this image a lot as a mental template, even if not exactly as written (Maslow)

most are familiar with it, u have the four levels of basic "stuff" like food and shelter and then some measure of respect/belonging and self-esteem

if u have all those, u are at the highest level -- u are in a self-actualized state

every day u wake up in this state, is a perfect day for u to get busy and make shit happen

because it doesn't get any higher...u are 100% all-systems go, ur visibility is as good as it will get.

2.

and so I would say esp if you're somewhere age 25-40, and have some nagging urge or tug, like

-"I want to do xyz but don't know if it's time"
-"I want to do x but it's risky"
-"I want to quit W2 and start a business"
-"I don't like my current life, I want to go live this other life, but doing so would involve risk, pain, discomfort, uncertainty"

these are all extremely common, everyone u will ever meet has felt some variation of one of those

and I shouldn't say 25-40, there are plenty of guys 40-60 who ponder this shit all day too...like, am I stuck in some fucking jerkoff ass job when I should be doing something else, because I know that my ceiling is so much higher?

thats why the intro Q -- "how do I know when its time?" -- was left intentionally vague.

time for what?

always time for something.

but at a younger age, u don't understand the rhythm of life yet, the boundaries and dimensions and guardrails

I am here to tell u that it does not get any better than where u are right now.

like, nothing more could align in your favor, God is not going to flip a switch for you

3.

if u were to ask "what more could I want to align, what am I missing?"

u will realize "there is absolutely nothing more I could need."

if ur healthy, relatively happy, and ur family and friends are happy and healthy as well, and u have no major pressing issues to deal with...

at the minimum, that qualifies as "a great day."

at the maximum, u should absolutely be launching yourself, like, immediately, at whatever u have nagging u

because its never gonna get any better than where u are, thats the thing.

I think its tempting to hold out some strange vague hope for...something? you don't even know what, but you're hoping, just, something?

never happens brotha, this is just procrastination or timidity or fear in ur pilot seat.

4.

so I'm not going to shove u off a cliff or write some dork ass post like "u should totally go do the thing bro, do the thing now, do hard things"

its more, gently calibrating ur frame of reference and mindset...kind of a "YOU ARE HERE" sign.

if you blast off, it will be from ur current position and lifestyle.

if u imagine urself in a helicopter, about to skydive, it's a matter of u just rocking forward and falling out of the plane.

that's where u are right now, that's the friction, tension, barrier that ur up against...100% mental.

theres no resistance beyond that

if tomorrow, u told everyone u know "hey I'm moving to Japan," you'd get a bunch of random comments and pushback

24 hours later, they've all forgotten and u wont hear about it again. 

no one cares. everyone has their own shit to worry about

u could already be in Japan 24 hours later, they don't know and don't care

5.

ur family, ur friends, they'll weigh in because they're obliged to, but its ur life and ur say is the final say

I say this because people freeze up worrying about what other ppl will think

its 24 hours and then its history

applies for literally everything...u can kill a guy and u will be front-page news, there will be 24 hours where u exist.

and then tomorrow...u go right back to being invisible to the entire world.

dont worry so much about what people think, and remember that ur execution shapes what they think

if u launch a billion-dollar Japanese business, everyone u know: "oh yeah I knew he'd do it...supported him, told him to go, always believed in him" et cetera

if u totally fuck it up: "oh yeah I told him it was a risky idea and not to go, I knew it was a bad move, tried to warn him"

people don't care

also, do ur own due diligence amongst people u know, who randomly moved across the world, took some crazy risk, etc...and ask them, "do you regret the decision you made?"

in my experience it is HEAVILY skewed towards "hell fucking no I don't regret it," even when it didn't pan out. because a lot of times, it shook off chains and cobwebs, and u re-centered how u think about getting the most out of life...things that u would just never ponder when u are in "white collar office" mode.

in both life and markets, u learn only thru action, thru paying tuition

should I trade software, go do 100 software trades and then review your P&L

same idea for most of life's "should I do X" questions

6.

now...the word "urgency" is tricky, but relevant

because time is not on your side

each year, the odds grow that u may end up with a massive fixed cost burden (mortgage/family/kids) which slams the "risk window" shut

u can still do it, people do it all the time, but "25-30 and single with no fixed costs" vs "40 with $200k in annual burn + humans relying on me"...definitely changes everything.

if its just you, your downside is extremely limited

worst-case scenario is something like, broke and unemployed and sleeping on a couch or in a shitty studio.

anyone older will tell u, "that really aint shit" lol

7.

u can cut ur costs to the bone, to just rent + food

somewhere between $3000 and $4000/mo, u can live a good life...a really nice studio is $2500 in south florida which means $2k or less in a lot of cities

so u need to make $40-50k per year to survive

anyone with half a brain can get there in 6-12 months, and after that, u will never work for anyone again. zero reason to, ur self-sufficient.

cutting ur expenses is everything, u can be living a $200k manhattan lifestyle one day, and the next, a $50k one where u live a mile or two from the beach

and u will realize "wait, why am I so much happier with every single day of my life even though my comp just went down 75%"

bc all the best things in life are free. sun, ocean, friends, books, writing, weights...keep shit super simple, minimum possessions, tight circle, and u are not only chilling, but u are chilling for life. once u have ur habits and ur friends locked in, u can ride those til u die.

8.

the digital nomad guys in their 20's and early 30's who make "internet money" and go live in new cities for 6-12 months on a stretch, bopping around LatAm and Europe, they've got it figured it out

that's exactly what u should be doing with ur life at that age, traveling, exploring, becoming financially self-sufficient where u can work from anywhere.

if I was 25-30 and lost/aimless I would just pick one of those guys who seems to have it figured out, offer to work for him for cheap, move somewhere cheap like LatAm or Vietnam, spend 2 years learning his playbook, and then go off on ur own.

"can I do that?"

dog its ur life, u can do whatever the fuck u want.  if u leave some $125k white-collarish office job, and within 2-3 years u can generate $80-100k on ur own

ur there, ur at parity, and all those mf's stuck in the office will HATE you for pulling it off.

but this is me talking, as if I was 25. idk if this is right for everyone

although I just can't see anyone saying "yeah I'd take the $125k city consulting job over the $90k of solopreneur fully-remote internet money"

a man, a laptop, LLM's and starlink. u can build fucking empires with that and u can do it from anywhere.

9.

the world changed so fucking much from 2020 to say 2022, and then accelerated again with AI.

u have to recognize, no one has a clue whats going on right now, this shit is wide open for anybody.

"you have to go work at these 4 firms and do this and that and take the metro north every single day for 40 years"

u dont have to do any of that shit

u have to make enough money to pay ur rent and afford food, and after that, its all blue sky

if you can scale to $150k over 5 years, and ur buddy is working at JP Morgan in NYC making $140k, like, how is anyone going to tell you shit lol

"I work better hours, I work for myself, I work from anywhere, and, I almost forgot, I make more money. What were u saying?"

conversation over

mogged from every possible angle

dont ever let ppl try and put u in some outdated box like "no u have to work at a NYC bank in ur 20's, bc thats what u do"...stuck in 2010 or some shit

the world changed in 2020 and again in 2022, toss the old rules out, go build ur dream life for urself.

END/LONG GAME

if u happen to have high expenses, u could still go get a job if u take risk and fail, or maybe u learned from ur failures and u run it back -- chances are u made connections with either "good ppl to work with/for" or "providers of capital"

if u have good people and access to capital u can just keep putting up shots over and over for the next 50 years, trying to build and create shit and learn from ur failures

if ur wired a certain way, this is an extremely fulfilling life, esp right now...one man and an LLM can launch literally ANYTHING into fucking orbit, anything

and u can easily find another 5-10 like-minded souls on X for you to link with, or build with, or brainstorm big dreams with

this is such a great time in history to be alive for a young scrappy entrepreneurial type who loves building and creating shit. 

there are so many ways to make crazy upside, that I can't see how u stay motivated at some "I'm gonna take my $100k flat comp each year...maybe a $5k discretionary bonus."

the notion of "taking risk" by leaving that setup, rebasing ur expenses to $50k, selling everything, and building a new life + multiple businesses from scratch...that does not sound "risky" to me, at all

once u scale to $50k income, the risk is gone, u made it, ur alive, ur self-sufficient, and u know what?

being able to say "for the next 50 years, I will NEVER get fired, because I work for myself"...is actually far less "risky" than all these finance/tech jobs where people are getting axed left and right.

--

that's all I've got on this topic for now...peep the graphic below, tape it to ur desk

remember, if u wake up, and u are in the top triangle -- self-actualization -- u are in "make shit happen" territory

and if u don't want to make shit happen, I'm fond of saying: "WHEN YOU HAVE THE CAPACITY TO HELP, HELP."

if you're healthy and happy and actualized, go be of service, go figure out how to be of use to someone, go help someone 5-10 years younger than you

some day u may be sick, or ur struggling with a major life issue, and guess what -- u won't be able to help anyone.

u don't have the capacity to help. so u can't help.

so...

"if you have the capacity to help, help."

Godspeed 😎

## 217. 2026-07-12
<a id="t-2076308917802394003"></a>

*Single post · [2076308917802394003](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2076308917802394003)*

on "complexity" as a core tenet in pitches

1/7

everyone has made this mistake

"underfollowed, misunderstood, overlooked, financials obscure X, obfuscate Y, multiple is low bc of poor disclosure, cross-holdings"

I could go on but I'll throw the fuck up from how much $ I've lost on ideas like that

1. Any pitch that starts with "underfollowed" should be shredded or cremated for ur own safety

the market cap is $5B dude

It's definitely followed

"misunderstood"

when you see that, the stock is in a drawdown measured in "football numbers"

I'll guess 80% on this one

(pulls up chart)

hmm....so its down 95%....and the bonds are trading at a 54% YTM

idk if it's misunderstood

would the bears say its misunderstood or just the bulls

plus, the superior r/r trade is to buy the debt and earn that juicy 54% YTM

Selected by Default, it says

the CFA exam spoke of this

the 54% YTM is selected by default, which means u lock in the yield

this is why u always want to move up in the cap stack, superior r/r profile

also, unless this was issued at a 54% yield, we can also get upside if it trades to par

the yield goes down and price goes up

that's what makes these "the fulcrum security"

the delicate balance of yield and price

Moyer spoke of this

---

2. jokes aside...a pitch based on complexity, without a CHANGE as to what will remove this complexity

is an awful pitch

excuse me, I should say...out of like, n=350

prob 200 stayed complex

100 became more complex (drew down 50%) and more misunderstood (chaptered out)

maybe 50 went up, although I couldnt give u a single ticker that went "complex" to "simple"

I spent 4 months learning Bollore and couldnt tell u what the fuck they do

there was a pitch by I think Muddy Waters, where the core of the pitch was:

"the market has the share count wrong"

I'm laughing because I was long at the time, and prob agreed

and because today, I cannot think of a worse bulletpoint to a pitch

like.... what is the catalyst?

"grok update the share count"

theres a 99.99% chance the market has the correct share count and just has a fat discount on the conglomerate

3. men will figure out what women truly want before they figure out Bollore's share count

bollore chart below...that CAGR is classic complexity

no one even knows wtf this company does

I think its a Somalian piracy roll-up with iron ore smelting exposure, and has dominant market share in Africa in both bribery and money laundering

this adds complexity and so shares trade at a compelling discount to NAV

we believe this is because the market views African bribery as lumpy and unpredictable

but if it traded at an American bribery multiple (Raytheon, Lockheed Martin, META/GOOG), we see 400% upside to shares

and if it traded at an American regulatory capture multiple (BRK), we see 800% upside

4. Bollore is known as the Buffett of France

No one has ever actually said this

however, there are similarities that make it an appropriate comp for valuation purposes, such as the letter B

and like Candyman, if u say it enough it becomes true

"Mohnish Pabrai is a comp for Charlie Munger"
"Li Lu is the Charlie Munger of Asia"

Munger is prob like, "I've never even heard of these fucking guys in my life"

Bollore offers a catalyst-rich path to realization of value

finally, we see an overhang removal by either RICO conspiracy indictment or INTERPOL prosecution

this would make the story less complex, I think

because it would force incremental financial statement disclosure

and the market will likely ascribe a new multiple, in-line with other Organized Crime Syndicates (JPM, WFC, Super Micro, First Brands, and Rick's Cabaret)

on a blended basis, we get to 15.4x EBITDA for 1200% returns

finally, we see ASR upside thru an underlevered balance sheet

if Bollore were to issue $500M in debt and use it to buy back stock, we see an extra 2.5% EPS upside

theoretically, they could just do like 10 of these ASR's back to back

so thats 25% EPS upside

where does the debt go?

dude

no one knows what the fucking share count is

what makes u think anyone knows their debt balance

look at that org structure...it looks like one of those Japanese pod hotels

there's gotta be room for this new issue somewhere, its just $5B

on the CFA exam, this was known as "a debt restructuring," I believe

this is where u just take debt and put it somewhere else

perhaps a debt restructuring is another bullish catalyst for the equity?

"wait... how are you pitching a RICO conspiracy indictment as a bullish catalyst?"

okay, so

it will force the market to reassess Bollore, as people type in BOL FP

this will draw new eyes to the name, and new analysis of their financials

everyone will ask: 

"wait, what is the true share count?"

boom, shares gap higher by 30%.

muddy waters spoke of this

---

5. 

realistically, what is a catalyst that would remove complexity....I dont know if I could point to one that would straightline to a higher stock price

have spinoffs and divestitures reliably created value or was the market pricing the value correctly within the enterprise?

so I dont know, honestly, I would say that unless you've been doing this for 15-20 years, stay the fuck away from these pitches

if its complex, its complex

it takes 10-15 years to understand why it appears cheap

but it takes 20-25 years to understand the bear view and why the market is pretty sharp at pricing complexity

if it has negative enterprise value, its prob not a free lunch

it takes 10-15 years to understand the negative EV, 20-25 to understand why the cash isn't valued at cash

shit like that.

6. "JapanCo trading at 2x! Complex, cheap, buy it!"

every pitch like that, without a reason WHY the 2x is wrong, and what will change the narrative....is a dud

"You're wrong, value is its own catalyst"

I just disagree with that so strongly lol

Because the market isn't fuckin dumb

the process vs outcome of those ideas, doesn't track

it went from 6x to 5x to 6x

was value its own catalyst at 5x?

who the fuck knows

7.

until then, avoid any pitch that is based on

"look at this undiscovered gem in plain sight...trades cheap bc of complexity"

interesting....Transdigm, I'll take a look at it, despite my concerns about the Compounder/Quality factor

at $73B mkt cap, it reminds me of Sanborn Map, or maybe See's Candy's

my analyst sees 20% upside based solely on blocking and tackling to reduce complexity, such as removing the "g" from Transdigm

like why is the g there

is it silent? is it a loud g? this complexity is weighing on the multiple

finally, we believe management should put out a press release confirming "we are not a Compounder"

also, may as well send an email to multi-mgr risk teams stating:

"dont put us in that fucking dumbass factor basket with the broken compounders. give us CAT's factors, CAT has good factors, give us the same ones...CTRL+C, CTRL+V."

END

if anyone can point me to a clean case study, of a complexity pitch in the last 10-15 years that worked for the right reason...I will write a follow up on this

I only remember graveyards

the malone complex from 2015-2020...a lot of financial engineering, complexity removals, and then 60% drawdown

"oh wait, LILA actually is a business...wtf"

the complexity of the org structure was offset by the complexity of latam cable/tv

and "revenues down 5%" went thru the fixed cost deleverage + highly-levered cap structure

this complexity was highly favorable, except for bears, culminating in a (50%) drawdown

CABO I believe was complex too, quite a chart, v complex, v misunderstood, v overlooked

last one...LBTYK chart, 14-year CAGR of (0%), this to me is exactly what complex situations do...and is why value is never its own catalyst.

for more from Bollore, follow BOL at: @BolBol

## 218. 2026-07-12
<a id="t-2076364169528406314"></a>

*Single post · [2076364169528406314](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2076364169528406314)*

Time To Redefine The Word "Compounder?"

1. Historically: book value and/or earnings CAGR
2. Post-GFC: 5-10 year TSR CAGR (10-20%)
3. Current: "QAAP Qapital LLC 3Q26 Investor Letter"

(Quality At Any Price)

Position Review:
>...the business continues to compound, CAGRing book value and EPS at 10-20%

>the stock is compounding as well, with a 4-year TSR of (65%).  

note: our belief is that absolute compounding rate matters more than short-term fluctuations in price

we recommend "Re-CAGRing" all returns using an advanced function in Excel. see below:

(Re-CAGR formula =ABS)

>we remain bullish on fundamentals and view this minor pullback as a buyable dip. our analysis indicates that the business compounds at 10-20%, and the stock has compounded at 10-20%, so, we see no reason why this should stop

>we did a deep dive into our top 2 positions, by saying "grok will these two compound at 10-20%? make no mistakes. note that we pay any price at QAAP. answer in words in english that are spelled Y-E-S." 

QAAP Valuation Discipline
>as a reminder, at QAAP Qapital, we believe there is no price for quality, because of a Munger quote we read a while back. he said a stock's return will equal its ROIC over long periods of time. 

>Similar to Buffett, we think as business owners and focus on long term ROIC. Modeling takes you away from qualitative discussions of quality, and quality compounding qualitatively when we compounded...hold on, I just blacked out, qualitatively. We believe that short-term valuation metrics distort quality compounding and we avoid all "Short-termism."

"What is a compounder?" 
>We have been mulling this over lately at QAAP Qap. Our businesses are compounding book value nicely, but the stocks are not, although on an absolute basis we are impressed with a 40% 1-year CAGR. 

Anything that can CAGR down, can CAGR up, and "CAGR Power" shows the compounding rate inherent in a business.

We think that right now, businesses should referred to as businesses, since its the rate of compounding that matters. 

While shareholder returns are nice, we avoid accounting metrics that arent related to quality.

So, going forward, we have decided that:

>sometimes, we'll just report the CAGR of book value or EPS
>other times, we'll report the TSR CAGR
>in case of emergency, we have decided to report an "emergency compounding rate." By utilizing an advanced Excel formula, the =RAND function will spit out a number been 10% and 20%.

In times of elevated market stress, we believe this "ECAGR" helps normalize for when compounding is disrupted.

We think this new definition of "Compounder" should help Quality investors remain grounded during short-term market blips.

>new position: Adobe Acrobat (ADBE). 

QAAP bought stock in Adobe Acrobat Company, the PDF maker with very high ROIC and a high compounding rate. Adobe is a great company known for their PDFs. PDFs aren't going anywhere and we were drawn in by such high rates of compounding that we couldn't help but QAAP our way into "quality on sale." 

We believe the AI threat is overblown and that a 20-year average ROIC of 80% is proof of a sustainable moat, driven by PDF growth and PDF usage. We are long term investors and view this 80% short-term fluctuation in price as a buyable dip.

>technical analysis is bullish. the Adobe chart is in a rare "reverse compounding" pattern which is clearly an aberration vs historical trends. our forecasts have this trend reversing into year end, with a normalization to a CAGR rate of 20-25% into 2027.

Factor Exposures

>our portfolio has been caught in a Factor Unwind

>the "CAGR" factor has been unwinding

>this has led to our stocks "Un-CAGRing"

>this never happens

>we have been adding factor hedges by buying more Lululemon every single day for the last 3 years. we think this Lululemon will add "ballast" to the book and provide much-needed CAGR to the portfolio.

-we also have bought more CHTR, as a factor bet on cable companies, which typically CAGR at 10-20%. we have been averaging down since 2021 in this position but view it as a buyable dip and offsets 

Market Inefficiencies

>We believe a pod blew up somewhere, causing them to liquidate our top 3 holdings by 75%.

>This unwind began in November 2021.

>It has been a series of daily blowups every day since then. 

>While 5 straight years of pod blowups are unusual, remember, those LOOOOOSERS at CitSec lost 60% in 2008 which is what happens when you do not abide by a QAAP-focused approach. 

>Are we surprised that pods blow up every day, no. Quality is its own risk management. When you own quality, you sleep well at night. 

>Rather than flitting around and dancing between the raindrops, like "Pointless72" does (our office nickname for those IDIOTS), we do what we do best: buy quality at any price.

Investor Day

We will be doing a remote investor day next Friday. Please join us as we explore a PDF, and explain why Adobe Acrobat is the 800-pound Gorilla of PDFs.

Until then, remember, absolute compounding is always what matters, not short-term up and down compounding. The =ABS function will keep you focused on quality, not on price. 

(disclaimer: all returns will be ECAGR until further notice)

## 219. 2026-07-12
<a id="t-2076399432577523842"></a>

*Single post · [2076399432577523842](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2076399432577523842)*

this is an excellent question, and one where I can give a serviceable answer for now

basically asking, "u tweet/write about breaking down tape, u post Kuechly breaking down tape...what is the market equivalent of this?"

1. grinding charts/screens -- like how Druck talks about how he gets 272 charts at 4:30 AM

2. P&L/trade blotter

these are both article-length answers

I will try and keep it brief here, forgive rushed writing/typos

P&L:

going thru all ur losses and troubleshooting. process vs outcome, luck vs skill, learning from ur mistakes

this sounds very simple, and I sat down to write a tweet-length answer....4000 words later was like, oh shit 

there's actually so many layers to it

but this to me is the most important, its the #1 way to improve, people who are serious do it

and yet no one does it 

because it sucks. it's you alone in a dark room by yourself, just staring at all ur bad decisions

2.

u want to know why 99.9% of people really don't do it?

they don't want to know the truth about themselves. the truth is ugly. they're afraid to look inside, because they don't know what they'll find.

and your P&L leaves you NOWHERE TO HIDE.

it is a mirror, and it's an unvarnished reflection of all your flaws, your bad decisions and your dogshit discipline

u cannot blame or make excuses, u cannot tweet around it, and it is immune to all forms of LARPitude

it's one of the clearest indicators of who is real not just as a trader but as a human

show me someone who is afraid to look at their P&L, and give me moneyline at -1000

lay $10k to win $1000 that they will never be successful in markets

its that simple

3.

if there were prop bets I'd tell u exactly how they blow their first account...time horizon, position sizes 

first off I've made every mistake myself, and second, over 10-15 years of trading, teaching friends/family, watching people fail in years 0-5 over and over and over...

I just know all the early-stage mistakes, especially the mental/cognitive ones, ego, vanity, denial, self-deceit, fear

someone in years 0-5 of trading markets is a fucking mess, like an asylum case...they underestimate how stupid, weak, and slippery the human mind is

4.

tell a guy "stop urself out down 20%" "okay"

takes a 60% loss

"what happened there chief? ur job was to see the number 20%, and then click the mouse....a 6-year old coulda done that"

mfs will either lose their shit, crumble, or enable some sociopath mode where the loss wasn't their fault

all people's fucked up childhoods and upbringings emerge in how they grapple with harsh truths

u see people who are knotted up too tight where u don't think they could ever be unwound

nothing is ever their fault, ever, and never will be

there are lots of people like this on twtr, in 2-3 sentences u can immediately discern how someone handles accountability and responsability

the human brain is frightening

so thats P&L.

1. grinding charts/running screens

I learned this from a boss back in the day, whose process wasn't too far off from this

Stan Druckenmiller: Okay, so every night, at about 4:30, I get 272 charts. They cover just about every group in the stock market, every commodity, every currency cross I'm interested, in every fixed income market around the world. And they're basically daily, weekly, and monthly. Daily supposed to predict 8-20 days, weekly 8-20 weeks, and monthly 8-20 months. When I make an investment decision, it's usually--sometimes, believe it or not: The idea comes from the charts, but I will never invest just on a chart. But if I really like a fundamental thesis and the chart stinks, I won't do it.

Druck trades macro, a L/S manager will have 500-1000 equity charts on top of that, diff time frames than Druck

you can do it post-close or pre-open

Druck and my boss were early AM, I always preferred doing it after the close

20-30 mins as a wind-down, just subconsciously uploading everything that happened during the day

here is a free screener I have always used

https://t.co/l8VF2TbITD

daily post-close, I do:

descriptive tab: "mkt cap > $300m"
technical tab: 52-week highs (1st screen) 52-week lows (2nd screen)

thats two diff screens, 52-week highs, and 52-week lows, both with $300m min (smalls mkt cap)

then, click "Charts" below all the toggles

this shows u where $ is sloshing in and out of, what's working, what isn't

u actually can use it for idea gen, because the market will always move first

before anyone has spilled a drop of ink, price has moved

everything is downstream of price

so when u see an odd move in a name that everyone has forgotten about, a name you knew well at one point, but that no one is talking about...

...and suddenly its printing 52w highs, on volume, on no news?

immediate "invest first and then investigate," in 24-48 hours you can get current

odds are always way better than 51-49 that fundamentals are inflecting, these trades are high hit rate because it's all incremental buyers from here

remember, no ink has been spilled yet

and there hasn't been any news

all u have is johnny-come-lately shorts getting taken out and the oddball deep value buyers, and now u prob have faster money, small L/S SM, prop desks, the minnows are there, the big fish

Druck talks about this but the fast, hard part of the move, is always when there's no news and no logic...u buy off instinct, bc u recognize the pattern, but u know that u are making a straight tape read with no fundamental work backing it

by the time the fundamentals justify the move that took place...half that shit is over

this is why u want deep familiarity, 5-10 years in the same sector

I haven't looked at something like Dillards in 8 years, 24-48 hours I'm current

whereas if you said "go learn Micron" I'm years away

once u understand a sector/industry, the financial statements pre-populate in your mind

this is how u counter the "invest first, investigate later"

like I have spent so many days of my life trading shit like apparel, u just develop familiarity with the way certain names trade

and newsflow hits, it gaps up 10%, and you've seen this movie so many times that ur gut says "buy it up 12%, now"

that response was created by the 4-5 times where I didn't buy it up 10%

weird subconscious shit that develops just staring at tape all day and all night

I run a few other screens too, all the sector ETFs like spdrs, plus the entire consumer cyclical subsector with mkt cap over $300m

prob takes 20-30 mins but over many years, u will silently upload info on reading the tape, what happens "under the hood"....how the S&P can be flat but there can be a sea change under the surface

I can't really explain why or how this works or why its worth doing, although if Druck is religious about grinding charts at 4:30 AM then there is prob some signal

I'll do a better job on these in an article but u should be good to go, and on Finviz, just play around with that screener and all the toggles and tabs

really great site, even when I'm paying for BBG I still just use finviz lol

BBG is too slow and clunky on a lot of shit, this site just zips

get used to just learning weird new tickers and chart patterns...whats this company, what do they do....ask LLMs and so on

a certain type of brain will go down this rabbithole without me telling them to

this is the shit that makes winners, winners

I hate to say it, because markets aren't the NFL

but it's the same principle...like, the guy who is putting in 1-2 hours daily, outside of work hours, grinding his P&L/blotter/losses, and grinding daily market action thru charts...

that shit compounds, everything compounds, over 5-10 years these daily efforts become jaw dropping

install these habits (and other good habits) and never break them, thats what determines success vs failure.....people with losing attitudes do not understand that every winner's story is this same sentence:

"A Ten-Year Overnight Success Story."

they see u in one day, on year 10, winning a title

they don't see the 10 years of staring at ur fucking losses every day

no one saw that

that was u, in a dark room, all alone

trying to fix what is broken in ur process, and what is broken within u as a man

u will discover that the two are the same

grab ur shovel and ur lunch pail...

because its hard hat szn🪖

good luck trading

> QT @Floureiro6: @gregoryblotnick Whats the investing version of watching game tape like this?

## 220. 2026-07-12
<a id="t-2076438713077228002"></a>

*Single post · [2076438713077228002](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2076438713077228002)*

(process vs. outcome) + (means vs. ends)

These two parallel ideas in mkts and life have to be understood

esp in markets, this is "major brain re-wire" territory

this is a lot more about attitude than anything else

1. in life, the moral precept is that the ends don't justify the means.

that's the "should" of how life should work...reality is more complex unfortunately:

i.e. "why do good men suffer while wicked men prosper?"

that's the same conundrum as ends/means

"my good means are getting me bad ends, and his wicked means are getting him good ends, wtf?"

that's life. but no philosophy for this post rn

just accept the moral precept of "ends dont justify means."

2. HOWEVER...markets are more nuanced.

I have not seen this made as a declarative statement, but I suspect that it would get the "yup" from the market sages and wise men:

"In markets, outcomes justify process."

A process is a good process if it generates winning outcomes.

The question with no answer, the nuanced part, is "what is the correct time horizon?"

3 years, 5 years, 7 years, 10 years?

When can you comfortably state that "these outcomes are driven by a consistent, repeatable process" as opposed to "a monkey throwing darts?"

A long time ago I uploaded 7 years as the answer, lost the source, but 7 years is the length of an average market cycle.

This means u get to see how a guy trades a bull market and a bear market. Until u have seen someone trade both, they haven't proven themselves.

You need the levered bull market geniuses to get wiped, and the overly conservative cash hoarders to underperform.

Whoever is left standing after that, with a solid-looking 7-year track...real-ass motherfucker, made man, O.G.

That logic is still sound to me, but I think the time horizon has shrank to 3-5 years, maybe less bc of how short a multi-mgr time frame is

or because more reps + more trades + more data to evaluate process/outcome = shorter required duration for the same amount of data...I don't know and the answer is clearly fluid anyway.

3. "Outcomes justify process."

I had to add the nuance at the beginning clarifying that this is not equivalent to means vs. ends

This is why people avoid making it declarative

But this is so important to understand from an attitude/mindset perspective

People who are results-focused, performance-focused, outcome-focused...just a different breed of human, winning attitude, same mindset as athletics

It's not nature, it's nurture

I know for a fact because ages 0-21 I had nothing going for me

You can take anyone and with 3-4 years of amazing mentorship, hazing really, u can turn a kid or a young man into a competitor, a legit human

I think that's why I feel so strongly about ages 20-25, like, I am just such a believer that in man's ability to destroy and rebuild himself at will, infinite malleability, the fact that u can become an entirely different person with 6-12 months of work

But this is just how people in any seat with P&L responsibility communicate

Twitter is not the real world, at all, I cannot emphasize this strongly enough

Go get coffee with the most high-powered person u can reach, somebody way up, and pay attention to everything about how they communicate

Like, day one, it's just made extremely clear by mentors, and then daily, by the market.

4. "This is not a career for people who make excuses."

That's basically it...because you are your P&L.

If you're down 20%, you are down 20%...like in the NFL, you are your record.

There is no "I'm down 20% but I'm good at this"

A good mentor will just wring this out of you immediately, like "you're down 20%, just shut the fuck up until your P&L doesn't suck."

There is no blame, 100% of everything falls on your shoulders

Ownership, accountability, responsibility

"I'm wrong" "I fucked up" 500 times a day

There's no such thing as "it's the market's fault"

Stuff like that is how you get dinged or fired, these are old trading floor rules from when all the world's best talent was on bank prop desks

I don't know if "military" is the right word, but it's not far off

5. No one cares about your process if your outcomes suck

If you have P&L to your name, "shut the fuck up until you perform" is not even rude or offensive, it's just a fact

Don't talk until you've earned the right to talk

Show ur outcomes, show ur P&L... that's what determines whether u should be talking

Until then, why are u talking at all? Why are u worrying about some other guy's P&L when yours is trash?

These are logical queries, this is not shit-talk...like, sit down and explain your thought process

In general, if I think across powerful industries, people I can recall who were at the top, law, business, et cetera

like, people are extremely fucking blunt, the higher up you get

they don't have much time, and they have a million mf's asking for their time

it almost takes you aback at first because you expect a CEO or a guy with $500M to be out of the movies or something

You get 2 word texts and a 24 second phone call, and he moves a mountain for u...next

thats how u get to that level, thats how u move.

6. One team, one dream

I had a boss who followed me on twtr back in the day, it was noon and it was a slow day, I sent a tweet

Man blew my goddamn brains out

Why the fuck are you tweeting, look at the P&L you fucking jerkoff, look at your names, and tell me if u think u have earned the right to fucking tweet during market hours.

Boom, smoked my ass, and he was 100% on the money

I said "I'm wrong" for the 100th time, course-corrected and didn't do it again

It's not just me, I'm letting the team down and being disrespectful

This is all de rigeur and u don't mind someone checking you if your attitude is wrong. It's one team one dream, thats the sort military-ish aspect.

In real life, "shut the fuck up until u have earned the right to talk" is just the right way go about, well, pretty much everything

I have never encountered a valid counter-argument to it

It will serve you well literally everywhere you go

7. All-weather

All your life you will have guys 10-20 years older than you evaluating you, from 20 to 40, 40 to 60, and so on

First impressions and the way you carry yourself are probably far more of it than we can even appreciate

When you wire yourself to move a certain way, u don't have to worry about how u come off

I respect elders for their wisdom, try to treat everyone the exact same, I try to speak in fewer and fewer words each day, more silence, more action... words are straight up worthless, u learn it more and more every day.

That's all I know about life

On words, just stare at a guy when he's talking, ignore what he's saying, watch his face...and listen to what your gut is telling you.

Literally gives u the clearest possible read about someone

I don't know a word he said

I don't know what my gut said

But like Bruce Wayne, u felt as if a bright light got blasted across the night sky...the ratsignal.

8. Humble and quiet 

All these principles, are they good or bad, I don't know

Probably 90% books and mentorship, 10% confirmation thru life

Most are screwed in pretty tight, just because I have been a lot of fucking places, and never seem to have issues with the basic shit, communication, respect, humility, attitude

I believe this is because everyone likes the humble, quiet guy who believes in paying dues and prefers actions to words

And on the other end, everyone despises the guy who hasn't paid dues but won't stop talking

Every time I meet someone I really respect and admire, I note that they use as few words as possible

So that's the direction I'm going in...silence is good for the soul.

END: INSTALLATION

In general, it's a very very simple way of operating to understand, should be an easy install, I feel like if you went on YouTube and searched military shit, it won't steer you wrong

And then in business its just that, plus two things

-you get assigned work

[pause, think abt when u can have it done]

then respond: "I will have this for you Tuesday by 10 AM."

get them that shit Tuesday by 10 AM and make sure its the best thing they've ever seen in their life

do that over and over and just shut the fuck up otherwise, and u will be literally everyone's favorite person

"when I give him something, he tells me when it'll be back to me, I can check that off my list, I don't have to follow up with him, and when I get it back, that shit is flawless."

senior mgmt is just sitting there, talking about you for 10-15 minutes behind closed doors

"Do we want him as CEO, CFO or COO?"

all there is to it brotha

when ur 20, ur like, wow those guys must be superheroes

then at 40 you see which of your boys got tapped

they are good at three things and three things only...

1. execution
2. execution

you'll never guess the third

3. execution.

u know the drill...

do good work, get good bread 🍞
