## 221. 2026-07-12
<a id="t-2076452454783242510"></a>

*Single post · [2076452454783242510](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2076452454783242510)*

My first boss always said:

"Bad color is worse than no color."

Meaning...he asks u "why is XYZ down 4%"

If you don't know, say you don't know.

Don't give some half-ass bullshit answer or something you're not certain on

Same idea as: "If you get on a bus going the wrong way, get off as soon as possible... cause its gonna take u longer and longer to get back."

no color = ur a blank slate

bad color = u have to UNLEARN wrong thought first....before u can even learn whats correct.

I bring this up bc like in the QT...ur mind is fertile soil, u gotta be careful what u plant in there

if u study heroes and conquerors, that's what will sprout

whereas a scrolling slopbeam diet...you know, I'm not even mad about memes and stuff, I love memes 

its that there are so many young motherfuckers just hanging on every single word, mostly biz/finance, as if it's all from the lips of God

like DMing u questions that could not have come from "no color"

bad color only

like, it would be like asking someone "why do baseball games have 124 innings?"

ur sitting there like, where did the fuck did u hear that

shit will have you just shaking your head

avoid this combo:

(confidence + ignorance)

hugely problematic

There's a reason I keep tweeting all these old fucking investing books

this is what I am saying: "This is good color. I am not good color. There's maybe 10 guys on here that will give you 100% good color....but read these books, in fact, these are the best investing color humanity has produced."

Spraying and praying life jackets everywhere, like, save yourself

I cannot lead u to Hyperborea, I am one man and not that bright or talented

Go read something written by a real player of the game, with real results and real respect on his name

One Market Wizards interview has the same informational content as 12 months of X

and the signal/noise ratio of twtr, I honestly feel like has just been straight down

but I don't like trashing X, this is really just a basic ROIC comparison

X you might unearth 5-10 bangers u loved, but it takes 3 fuckin hours

https://t.co/yfY30NR12V - this shit is like the Bhagavad Gita in comparison, straight shots of Everclear vs. a Michelob Ultra

whats the ROIC on "densely packed compendium of everything the greatest investor knew"

Buffett would tell u its all about opportunity cost

What's ur next best option?

Precisely...lol

bad color is worse than no color fam

protect ur time and protect ur mind

as GZA said on Legend of the Liquid Sword, flipping a classic slogan...

"A rhyme is a terrible thing to waste."

> QT @gregoryblotnick: shout out to anyone who has stopped caring entirely about being liked

stuff u learn at 40, which would've been nice to know at 25... yet could not be known ex ante.

why?

because u don't know who ur friends are at 25.

once u nail that down... u should naturally arrive at this destination.

1/5

u take a look around, grateful, having come to understood two things:

I. loyal friendship is one of life's most beautiful blessings

II. loyal friendship is rare

after u read enough old books, u recognize that having 5-10 loyal friends over the course of one's lifetime would be outrageously good luck.

u count ur blessings, u stop chasing what u dont have, and u grow infinitely more grateful for what u do.

it's almost like coming out of a haze

2/5

u look at your 1000 social media "connections," u recognize that there's nothing for you there

u have enough friends where u can completely stop caring, from a position of strength, of peace, and of plenty

u would always prefer to be liked than to be hated.

and u know that the wise man avoids incurring hatred wherever possible.

but ultimately...u just don't care.

u feel driven by an obligation to pay it forward and be helpful to the younger versions of urself, just as others helped you 15 years ago

whether u are liked or hated is irrelevant to fulfilling this obligation

3/5

strangely, by expending 0 energy on being liked, u may end up with better results.

a wonderful example of this is John Adams' defense of the British

taking the course of action that he KNEW was going to lead to bloodcurdling hatred leveled in his direction.

this is why u cannot care about being liked or disliked, thats not ur north star...courage is

the great man finds himself at a crossroads frequently... remain silent and be liked, or do what he knows to be right, and give it no further thought beyond that

if u read history, u know that posterity will always vindicate the correct decision....that's your time horizon, not whether it's liked today.

4/5

last is losing all desire to fit in

this, again, is driven by the "eminent dead"

it appears binary to me every time

"u can either fit in, or u can be great."

u are either walking a crowded path into the crowd, or walking your own path into the forest.

the "lone wolf" archetype that Munger uses for himself

not to be confused with a misanthrope

people, in my experience, when u get them 1 on 1 behind closed doors...are universally good.

you can have a warm heart, a tight inner circle, a very high bar for ur time, and a preference for solitude -- all simultaneously.

5/5

with all of this, u aren't actively trying to not fit in, or to fit in

u just don't care

u tend to have no idea where u are relative to everyone else's opinion

u may be 100% in-line or 100% at odds

because this is the way.

think of how Buffett values a business on its own, with no market inputs.

END

here is a worldview that I feel is healthy and balanced

>the great man thinks critically and independently
>groupthink and crowdthink are toxic pollutants
>the mind is fertile soil and extremely sensitive to the inputs u feed it
>feeds and scrolling are poison, and u feel them destroying ur critical thinking capability

u simply have to protect your serenity, and it takes an active effort to preserve your peace. Nietzsche in this tweet below arrives at a similar conclusion

if I had to sum this entire approach up, I would say:
>u want to blend solitude and desire for independence
>u do not aim to be in or out, liked or disliked, and at all times, u retain a warmth and gentleness towards your fellow man as well as a desire to be of service with what little wisdom u possess
>u hold a polite and respectful apathy for the view of the crowd, and u do not aim to be in or out...your view is developed independently first, and may or may not dovetail with theirs second.

lot in there, my friend...I hope it at least helps u in thinking differently.

many high level and operating system changes one could make -- starting with yourself, and then the way u approach life, the crowd, society, and your fellow man.

whatever u do, "never let ur warm heart turn cold."

paz hermano 😎

## 225. 2026-07-13
<a id="t-2076661949048209624"></a>

*Single post · [2076661949048209624](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2076661949048209624)*

paragon (cont)

re: random DM q's, just highlighting more shit the pitch does well, starting at top

intro: format

esp "what abt format"

there is no format, anything goes really

fair question if ur young... u want it to look good

but lot of times, its just u and 1-5 people talking, for hours, the "format" isnt a thing except in an intvw

a lot of these VIC pitches are straight text and they're effective

generally though, I think a good north star is: a good pitch should be a LEAN 2 pages and every paragraph punches them in the face - put all the fluffy shit, tables, graphs after that

if u can write 2 pages of straight lasers like this, u can write text

but for 99% of analysts, this would've taken them 10-20 pages to get their point across (and not been nearly as good)

the only way you can write a "text model" like this is from having spent 500 hours staring at Excel, he clearly knew Noble, knew the assets, had familiarty with all this

note how there's no fat, there's no two-paragraph business description

not even "paragon sells rigs"

in one sentence u get the entire setup and it works

if u can write one sentence that good, then do it

u don't need all the formal "variant view" etc,

he does none of that, but its all included in there

best way to frame it is prob, "I have 7-8 paragraphs to make my point, what do I want to talk about in each one, how do I write this shit so hard and tight that by page 1.5 he's buying puts on his phone"

1. maint/growth capex, there's just no other way to have that level of conviction than 5-10+ years doing primary research on the subsector.

and not just developing variant view on maint/growth split, but "here's why mgmt's guide is wrong"

not important where he worked at the time other than, top-tier SM, europe based -- but thats rsch driven by both corp access + primary rsch

so if ur 20 years old u are not gonna write a pitch like this

tricky thing w/ maint/growth capex is u never know when you'll get paid, or if its priced...it affects FCF, but there's no catalyst that shifts mkt perception

esp bc maint capex isnt always disclosed

and then the pitch relies heavily on mgmt being idiots, which can go other way esp on short pitches (very easy to get hyperbolic), but I have no criticism here

but I wouldn't put like "they sounded dumb last transcript" as core of the pitch...its pretty clear that here, he just knows the whole space and how paragon sounds relative to rest (overly optimistic, poor operators)

so on maint/growth/fcf, how to think abt it re: event path, I don't know

but a pitch that goes right into fcf is never wrong, the guy that is fucking around with ebitda multiples is the one who is wrong

this is a far superior analyst to me, and prob 99% of people I've ever met, I blindly defer to him

you know what an ebitda based pitch doesn't take into account?

maint/growth capex

some dumbass prob was flexing 3x, 4x, 5x ebitda on this enterprise (bull or bear)

zero variant view

thats "average" analyst, this is a great analyst

2. all throughout the pitch just running down the ebitda to fcf math as variables

ebitda less capex less cash int less cash taxes gets u fcf

can default to that always, it works, its fundamentally sound, and u avoid messy/immaterial CFO stuff

cash int and cash tax wont change, u are just flexing ebitda and capex

and so as u analyze or adjust line items (revs, cogs, opex) ur right there

3. the rev components, most will go over the head of anyone who doesnt cover the space, but u can see rev = dayrates * utilization

its hard from the outside to see what drives his assumptions but idk if it matters

bc all throughout, he comes at ebitda from several angles.

the model might start at ebitda, a lot of models do (built out by segments, segment ebitdas all flow to main tab ebitda, and u flow down to EPS from there + use the same capex/cash int/cash tax as before, bang, FCF

when you know a company cold, that's what a 10+ year exp L/S analyst buyside model looks like

there's 3-4 variables that matter (as in this pitch) - the BS/CF, whatever

here, it prob goes to ebitda and then the rev math is a sanity check, with individual rigs broken out as a 2nd sanity check above revs

i've seen these models, they are a mess, u model all these individual rigs

but trying to flow 40 rigs into total rev is silly -- and all the warm/cold stacking, u model that, its GIGO

so something like this, esp where u are playing for a terminal outcome...

"better vaguely right than precisely wrong"

the way hes does it is great in the sense of, we have certainty in capex int and taxes to the point where ebitda being 400, 500, 600 or 800...u have a lot of room to be wrong

just constantly running bull/base/bear on everything as u go through it, and u dont even need to be laying it out fancy model style

here's base, if we sensitize x y we get z, other way we get zz

that's how you turn 1 page of text into 1 paragraph

10 page pitch should be 10 paragraphs, cut two, you have a lean 2 pager

4.  fcf

the fcf/dcf math is simple here and unfortunately not every stock is so easy to run in ur head

in any case here u only have a few years fcf remaining so u can sensitize it the way he does

i'm not sure i've seen a pitch on a stable company that could do a "paper dcf" I guess you could call it...if fcfs are predictable then you probably could

but the pitch is fundamentally sound...no P/E * EPS, like, these are the fcfs

mkts price off fcf, not eps

there's asset value for co's like these, he deads that angle and keeps going...the pitch pre-empts all the questions that a PM would ask

"could they do xyz"

a good short pitch has all the bull tenets available (from street) so if you can just take a machete and slice shit to pieces for 8 paragraphs, thats gonna be a good pitch

5. capital structure

almost absurd to read

this is one of those pitches where you are so far away from consensus, and from market pricing, that you have to wonder wtf you're missing

sitting there like "why isnt the market cap 0 right now, what could I be missing"

I'll tell you what you're missing

its called conviction, and an appetite for risk

they go hand in hand

but this is just a high-conviction pitch, like everything is sealed the fuck up

there are so few analysts who even have the balls to write a pitch like this, much less get it right

"the equity is a zero and the debt is a good short" is an outrageous bar

like u better nail this fucking call, because u are straddling the line on cocky vs confident

pitching a fucking equity short with such conviction that at the end, u sprinkle in that this equity is so fucked u can short the bonds

bygone era, u cant do that at a multi

and this pitch wouldnt exist at a multi...prob too illiquid I'm guessing

and you'd have to own something against this squiggly little levered equity turd

btw, if u look at the stock chart, I think this IPO'ed at $18 and went to $9

so imagine the chart

down 50% in a straight line

u walk in with ur pitch "short it down 50% because its going to zero, press it all the way down and also short the fucking debt"

if u are a L/S analyst, ur gut instinct shivered right there at the thought of shorting something after its off 50% in a straight line

too many scars from shorting something down 40% into a print and it goes up 50% on a miss

much less some shitco like this where the fundamentals are bad, but if the backdrop changes, this shitco equity stub will rip your fucking dick off

you see these 2-3x off the lows on just a small move in oil, crowded positioning, no real change in fundamentals

but thats it bro, I don't remember seeing any other VIC pitches that were as sharply written. everything about this would be fine as a multi-mgr pitch

most VIC pitches are snoozers, like NVR homebuilders

"we see book value compounding at 23% for the next 5 years"

useless in multi mgr L/S

end

somehow I forgot

the very first line being "price target = $0"

hero shit

courage and conviction are what separate an elite analyst like this from the rest, every PM will smile on these traits bc no one has them

u think some kid that just came from KKR to Millennium is gonna slam his dick on the table with a pitch like this

he doesnt know what "bid-ask" is yet

think thats it...if I missed anything DM.

> QT @gregoryblotnick: this stock pitch is pretty close to 10/10 imo, from VIC

Short - Paragon Offshore by Biffins (2014)

2 pages, thats how long these should be. and u know why its so short?...because he knows this motherfucker COLD.

"IPO to Chapter 11" speedrun hall of fame.. 6 months?

smoked it https://t.co/QIKLLkVFQM

## 227. 2026-07-13
<a id="t-2076785601672609976"></a>

*Single post · [2076785601672609976](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2076785601672609976)*

On breaking down tape/P&L

main thing to do to learn and improve off ur own red P&L

be disciplined in tracking ALL individual events where u took swings, esp binary setups, any discrete catalyst

or trading FOMC or AAPL day or pre mkt or post or lockup day trade

trading AM, PM, futures, options

the reason is that ppl will keep participating in setups, or say “ya they work and I’m good at them”

and there’s no way they have the data

what’s ur win loss, lifetime P&L, specific on the event itself (entry to exit)

it’s not “oh I had the idea”

it doesn’t exist on twtr but IRL, it’s called execution

and it’s much harder than idea gen

but no one has the reps, the data, no one was tracking it. I don’t care but ur just bs’ing urself about being data driven 

I think, tho, that may excel at d’s

deeznutz that is 

goes for all fundamental ideas too…u find ur circle of competence thru trial and error, via negativa

finally, u want to assign everything to one of these four squares to determine if u were right or wrong, lucky or good

this is how u rapidly improve, this is the process of a winner - munger’s rubbing of one’s nose in ur mistakes

this the real deal, this is how u see kuechly making them plays

and guess what

absolutely no one does even a second of it

legit 99.5% never grind P&L

and that’s why they make zero progress

they know their P&L is trash, they’re fundamentally driven and their P&L is littered with 2DE’s and 25% overnight gaps

this is why no one tweets P&L here

“Ya I was long MU all year”

YTD P&L: down 46%

Two earnings plays (expired worthless) and a 0DTE

P&L YTD just craters and land mines..,in a stock up 500% YTD

that’s why there’s pnl

But this is called EXECUTION and it is hard as fuck

I wrote everything I know on the subject in there and I made it into a shit test….8000 word gauntlet dedicated to the 0.5% of ppl who are serious about trading markets

this is what separates the good from the great

u gotta be doing it like kuechly

Because all greatness…it always comes from being in a dark room, all alone, grinding ur nose in ur mistakes

loving the basic blocking and tackling

invisible to the world

shout out to all those who stuck to the code this year

told u that shit is magic…everybody loves the motherfucker who shows up, doesn’t say a word, hands u machine quality work, and goes home

executing to perfection
soul of the hard hat aura  🪖

> QT @gregoryblotnick: The Final Frontier: Breaking Down Tape, P&L Mirrors, Emotional Trench Warfare

---

## 228. 2026-07-14
<a id="t-2076982920221212925"></a>

*Single post · [2076982920221212925](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2076982920221212925)*

WSJ in May 2000 on Druck/Soros getting headfaked by tech stocks…shows u how literally some of the greatest to ever do it get bedeviled by the mkt + let px action override conviction

esp the downsides of Druck’s style - which, as tends to be the case, are his greatest strengths (flexibility, willingness to change his mind on a dime, tape feel)

example:

“Though he did a bit of selling at the beginning of 2000, he held on to most of those inflated tech stocks, betting that "the Nasdaq rally was in the eighth inning, not the ninth inning." Few underlings challenged him. "Stan admitted to me that he didn't quite understand the entire story and was uncomfortable with valuations," says Richard Eakle, an outside money manager who took part in Soros internal conferences this year. "But everyone was intimidated by Stan. It was a group of yes-men at the meetings."

—

and then this…keep in mind both were pounding the table bearish tech a year prior

—

The Soros-Druckenmiller dissension came to a head over VeriSign , an Internet-security company that the funds bought at $50 a share last year and rode to $258 by late February. At Mr. Druckenmiller's behest, the funds doubled their bet on VeriSign to $600 million in early March, after a visit by VeriSign Chief Executive Stratton Sclavos. The CEO wowed Soros executives with talk of what a pending acquisition of Network Solutions could do, say people familiar with the visit.

VeriSign was at $240 when Mr. Druckenmiller doubled up. As the Nasdaq quaked, the stock fell to $135 by early April. Mr. Soros told his deputy, "VeriSign is going to kill us. We should take our exposure down."

"No," Mr. Druckenmiller replied. "This is different than other Internet plays."

It wasn't. VeriSign's shares fell to $96 in April”…

—

Druck is the man and is idolized for good reason by everyone

But u never wanna be blind to someone’s flaws, his style is still his style, and that style took him out of the game in April 2000:

“As for Mr. Druckenmiller, "It would have been nice to go out on top, like Michael Jordan," he said at the news conference 10 days later. "But I overplayed my hand."

—

only one guy had the courage to stick to his convictions all throughout this period, even with the whole world laughing at him

the peanut brittle muncher of omaha

and that trait is how he earned his goat stamp 🐐

(article: https://t.co/2f8BQbnTqa)

## 229. 2026-07-16
<a id="t-2077694278009049316"></a>

*Single post · [2077694278009049316](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2077694278009049316)*

shout out to everyone who just sends a DM like

“ur the man”

and that’s it...maybe an “fyi” in there also. such a good way to be + ends up saying a lot of positive things about ur character

bc that’s the convo, it ends there. I know u don’t need anything or want anything more.

just a very genuine and sincere message of gratitude...u usually get it after going the extra mile in writing something

I try and go 50-50 markets/life, in mkts it would be like some long-ass post on modeling, or in life something deep/painful where u make urself vulnerable in a way that connects with them and provides balm for a troubled mind

I should prob send more of these dm's but I think its all part of one big pay-forward, the chain of command...I used to send a lot when I was younger...now I just reply with some combo of "no, ur the man" (bc u are, for sending that) and "when its your turn, pay it forward."

but either way...like, keep doing that, its a wonderful attitude. the world needs a lot more "ur the man" guys 🫡

## 230. 2026-07-16
<a id="t-2077697419064525209"></a>

*Single post · [2077697419064525209](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2077697419064525209)*

v common twtr error/misconception to be cleared up

lot of times you’ll see “XYZ reported $3.63 in EPS vs street $3.71….shares sold off 15%”

and there’s always guys like “lol at the market selling it down 15% on a 8 cent miss, so irrational and stupid”

but u cant just go off the headline or even what u see in news/media

there is a lot of "analysis" like this, what I'd call "never-leave-twitter" analysis, just reacting to headlines

u gotta go to the company IR site and read the press release + any supplemental materials.

a lot of times when the qtr EPS and guide look okay, but the stock is reacting, its not the market being silly...its deeper than that

never assume "the market is being silly" when something moves 10-20%. when shit is in-line stocks tend to trade in-line.

there are obviously exceptions for this (as there are for anything in markets) but over and over u will be amazed at how little work people do, lazy/incorrect read throughs, cant even be bothered to go read a press release

think independently, do ur own homework

remember..."BAD COLOR IS WORSE THAN NO COLOR."

---

## 231. 2026-07-16
<a id="t-2077701713599856947"></a>

*Single post · [2077701713599856947](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2077701713599856947)*

on breaking down tape...another really valuable exercise

"what would I have had to believe at the time for ___"

esp for situations that u missed or sized poorly

pull up the chart AT THAT TIME - truncate it, dont look at it from today's viewpoint

like in that paragon pitch, I pointed out how the stock had just gone $18 to $9 in a straight line

for a lot of longs that you "missed," there was almost always a parabolic/exponential chart, or an earnings print where you had to buy a +20% gap

the more u do this, u will stop bullshitting urself on the difficulty of "riding winners in size"

what would your model have had to look like?

usually, you would've been 50-100% above consensus, a variance so wide that you'd start questioning reality

same deal, like, stop thinking that pulling massive P&L out of a single position is easy, conviction and peter lynch's "stomach" are insanely hard to develop

like Buffett riding a 30-40% AAPL position for 7 years or whatever, dont bullshit urself into thinking ur built like that...its so hard to develop this intestinal fortitude

remember, this exercise doesn't even factor in sizing, u can do all the tape breakdown, but u need to visualize the live P&L and how it would affect u emotionally

to me though, this is really what separates the great from everyone else

just sitting there on a Sunday afternoon running "what would I have had to believe" scenarios, over and over

building up their pattern recognition

so that when the elephant finally shows up...and others are freaking over the chart, or model variance, u arent fazed...like Kuechly talking about how the game slows down. gun is loaded, cocked, and u bag that mf 🔫

---

## 232. 2026-07-16
<a id="t-2077709550916952094"></a>

*Single post · [2077709550916952094](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2077709550916952094)*

on reading & tweeting

always trying to find the right balance, theres a ratio, like ur kill/death ratio...u prob want at least 1:1 reading

and that assumes u use twitter for writing practice and not just 100% zombie slopbeam mode (in which case the ratio has to be higher)

so why there is a beedrill pokemon card

because for me...I can't do both constantly (reading/tweeting)

like if I'm on twtr all day, and I try to read at night, my focus or something is fried, I retain nothing

I dont know "why" this is and I dont question it, I just take it for what it is...social media poisons ur mind

it results in u reading surface-level/superficial BS, and writing/commenting on surface level BS, ur outputs will always reflect ur inputs

I dont think my mind is unique here and I think 100% of people are affected the same way, but they dont stop and analyze it

if I open twtr in the AM, that day is shot...if I send a tweet, that day is shot

so right now, what I've found that (seems to) work, is u have to do 2-3 days on, 2-3 days off

shut it down at some point friday and pretend it doesnt exist til sunday PM or better yet monday AM

if u need news over the wknd, go to a news site (wsj/bbg), u are missing nothing besides that

this is the only way I can get any real reading done...long blocks over the wknd

and then you'll usually end up generating a long list of shit to write about, so u can later go 100% in the other direction (all writing/zero reading)...bc ultimately the point of all the reading is to generate something from it

so now both ur inputs and outputs should be at a deeper level, and at some point, if u keep doing this, u may end up writing something worth a shit...or at least thats what I tell myself

so if ur struggling to read and/or retain what u read -- ie you turn the page and realize u didnt read a single word of the prior page, stuff like that -- u may as well start here.

## 233. 2026-07-16
<a id="t-2077745429710606477"></a>

*Single post · [2077745429710606477](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2077745429710606477)*

on making asks of other people's time...this is actually sort of a life skill, its not just "I'm 21 and need help"

turns out that u always need help from whoever is 10-20 years older than u

the form of help changes but I'm 39 and sometimes seek advice/wisdom from ppl in 50's/60's

it happens less and less with LLM's, and also with experience, because the most important "problems" = you vs the man in the mirror

u either execute or u dont.

ppl will waste years dancing around that hard truth, asking other ppl what to do, instead of asking themselves "what the actual fuck is wrong with you" and then fixing it

ur red P&L has all the answers

I'm pretty sharp-elbowed w/ ppl my age on this re: load-bearing delusions or "u are running from urself"

but if u must make an ask on someone else's time, my humble opinion is that its best done in the most direct form and with as few words as possible

I am absolutely not an authority on this, and I only know the world I've dealt with (pms/traders, fund mgrs/founders) who tend to be blunt communicators

but at the same time, all people who are high up, are busy as fuck.

its safe to assume that between work and family and trying to not be a shitty person (aka responding to all the random DMs/emails asking for help)...most guys are stretched thin, usually inches from a breaking point

direct quotes are like: "I literally just want 1 free hour to lay on the couch in my boxers and stare at the fuckin ceiling in silence."

thats where most ppl are at

so I've never seen these vagaries to be received all that well
"would love to pick ur brain"
"would love to hop on a call sometime"
"would love to meet for a coffee chat"

like bro WHAT DO YOU WANT

its not an unwillingness to help, so much as, this can be resolved in a 1 minute text/email or 3-5 min phone call

especially with LLM's now which generate these super fucking flowery messages, they have a way of saying in 4 paragraphs what shouldve been one sentence

so its prob better to err in the other direction, where at the minimum they'll say "I like ur style kid"

watch how jamie dimon communicates...to me, he's a textbook example of preferred style amongst ppl "way up," in any industry.

ultimately, I dont think any of this is new

the world tends to reward the person who knows what they want and isn't afraid to ask for it... this 60 second video of steve jobs, it prob says everything that needs to be said on the subject.

## 234. 2026-07-16
<a id="t-2077753438960529525"></a>

*Single post · [2077753438960529525](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2077753438960529525)*

the "come-up" isn't a 1x thing so much as it is a process that repeats itself, a series, just with higher and higher stakes as u move up the layer cake.

it would be a lot more convenient if life was just up-and-to-the-right the whole time

this is never the case

looks more like a squiggly ass line w/ pitfalls and setbacks and cruel twists of fate that blast u onto ur ass

and from each of those, u are forced into a new come-up

if u study those who seem to have a decent knack for life (the "eminent dead") there are 2 things they get right

one is they reduce the blast damage + rarely take Critical Hits

this is old stoicism shit, how the same event can hit 1000 men, but each of those 1000 processes the event differently + reacts differently...temperament, resilience, thick skin, "freedom to choose one's response to stimulus" (frankl)

unavoidable that life puts u on ur heels, but controllable = degree to which it knocks u back + time to stabilization.

and then 2nd, is the ability to generate momentum from a standstill

kind of like the standing long jump in the olympics

those two things...are what close one chapter and begin the next, as ur series of "come-ups" continues on.

## 237. 2026-07-17
<a id="t-2078034745296908510"></a>

*Single post · [2078034745296908510](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2078034745296908510)*

FAQ on "breaking down tape" for fundamental L/S -- why do it, what does grinding thru charts teach u, whats the framework/philosophy to understand, what are u looking to solve for, how does this aid u as fundamental analyst/PM, and how can LLMs help u master this?

Part 1 of 5: WHY

I. Helps answer Q: "What is the market telling us?"

II. Grinding thru charts each day after mkt close (or pre AM next day) helps develop pattern recognition, and understand interplay b/w news and px (and how this changes in various regimes --> can be used to spot regime changes)

III. useful for risk mgmt, entry/exit, avoiding trades driven entirely by momentum or buying "falling knives," also v useful for idea generation (longer topic)

IV. part of a disciplined rsch process, esp in a coverage model... grinding thru charts is how you spot individual names that seem dislocated or may have signals within unusual px action.

II. ORIGIN

1. this is not my creation, this was taught to me by a former PM (process was very similar to Druck's re: having an early-morning "chart pack" with hundreds of charts, 200-300 macro, all bonds/energy/commods and more) plus anywhere from 250 to 1500 U.S. equities -- usually 250-500

2. Many PMs are "chart first" the same way Steve Cohen is, or PTJ, or other trader-types...fundamentals matter to all of them, but even Druck says no fundamentals can make him buy an awful chart. this is just pragmatism/risk mgmt

3. I generally haven’t seen this practiced by many analysts, or written up online as "good practice" despite being fairly commonplace amongst L/S PM's - not sure why this is

4. Its most useful for anyone who has some grounding in fundamentals, but who needs to learn “market feel” - so even if u have banking/PE experience and are above-average on financial statement analysis, you start from zero here, just like everyone else.

III. PURPOSE

1. not just "what is the market telling us" but "what is the market regime RIGHT NOW, what is the current trend?" It doesn’t tell us what’ll happen tmw. Neither do fundamentals, if u havent noticed. Nothing does.... nothing "works," nothing is bulletproof.

2. However, momentum is the most powerful force in markets, and understanding today, is not going to hurt in thinking abt what happens tmw. Also, helps develop an understanding of mkt regimes and narrative as primary drivers of px in short-term, as well as capital flows/liquidity, all of which are a very real part of the game.

3. In times like these where nothing makes sense (too many cross currents), too many opinions, too much noise…”dont read news, read tape.” The market always moves first – everything you read is downstream from this. If u are confused about the mkt right now the absolute worst thing you can do is scroll Twitter. 

4. Everyone here is repeating 3rd and 4th hand interpretations of tape from some other guy, or ignoring it entirely. 

How many tweets have you seen in the past 3-12 months "wow the market is being soooo stupid and offering great names down 20%, sharpen ur pencils boys," and now they're all down 50% with the SPX at record highs, not once asking "maybe the market is right and I'm wrong?" 

Point being, just go right to the tape urself and form ur own opinion...is the tape perfect, no, nothing is, but its the best we've got. 

IV. PHILOSOPHICALLY

1. Capital duration dictates time horizon and thus reliance on px action. If you're Buffett, you don't care about charts as much (if at all) as 99.9% of asset mgmt. If you're in multi-mgr L/S, px action matters and there's no way around it, as do sentiment, positioning, and so on. I am guessing you aren't working at Berkshire Hathaway and so charts will help with all things px action.

2. Paying attention to single-stock eps reactions (which you'll see breaking down tape) helps gauge underlying health of a stock, a sector, and as a broad mosaic, the market

3. When u are learning a new stock, study the last 10-20 years of the chart, u want to know everything that happened, all the big moves, and what drove them (bc history doesnt repeat but it always rhymes). If a stock has gapped 30% on earnings multiple times, it can and will happen again...some businesses are more conducive to these moves than others.

4. Druck quote: “the single best forecaster of the economy is the inside of the stock market.” Inside meaning, internals (longer topic), sector rotation, leadership, breadth, lots of "under the hood" measures... not just what the SPX does each day. 

V: HOW I LEARNED

1. When I say "mentorship," there usually is no such thing. A PM wont be looking over your shoulder. If you're in the same room as them and get to listen to them as they think thru shit, you are very lucky, and that's a true mentorship even if its indirect.

2. The best mentor is a crazy motherfucker bc they talk to themselves out loud all day. This is an A+ seat if u land it. I’m crazy fwiw so crazy is a term of endearment.

3. These chart packages had to be ready around 5-530 AM, printed out, on his desk, which was when he walked in. Youngest guy on desk had to do this which was me at the time. So that meant being in very very early swapping names in and out of various packs, updating "current positions" chartpack, and so on.

4. After doing this every day for years, it naturally became a part of my process, you just get used to knowing how every single name you cover is trading (in my case consumer). Only difference is that some PM's prefer to do this pre-mkt, I'd rather stay late and spend 30 min anytime between 4-8 PM grinding thru charts...mornings always have enough going on.

after 10-15 yrs of doing this daily, "breaking down tape" compounds and u end up with better market feel than other ppl u know.

NEXT STEPS:

I originally wrote this template up FAQ style/organized bc I was gonna do a video on it, but honestly, I'm posting it here bc I think u are going to be better off with our good old friends the clankers.

Using LLMs for Mastery: you can ask LLMs abt this just as you would anything else...you want to learn/understand:

high level
>market internals
>understand sector rotation, cyclicals vs defensives
>understand mkt leadership (broad vs thin), breadth
>understand breakouts and breakdowns and what they signify
>understand volume and gaps
>understand incremental buyers and sellers, liquidity,  outsized effect on price when a stock is crowded into a trend reversal, muted effect on prices when a stock is "sold out" / capitulation

idea gen
>understand relative strength and weakness, not the technical indicators like RSI, but just eyeballing "this stock trades unusually well compared to sector/index"

>understand how during a market selloff, to find the stocks that are trading the best / showing signs of institutional support. these will be the leaders of the next leg higher (long idea gen).

>this works in reverse as well. right now the SPX is 1% off all time highs. if some high beta dogshit is already down 40-50%, and then the entire market rolls over, the names that were weak in a strong tape will lead to the downside in a bearish tape (short idea gen).

[aside: risk mgmt/current tape]
>with all these charts...u want to understand how to LISTEN TO THE TAPE.

>I have to just comment with ES/NQ rolling over rn, there has been so little humility on display its incredible. I have seen 1000 tweets where someone is sitting on a (55%) loss and will just laugh and say "oh yeah, this stock disconnected from fundamentals a loooong time ago -- can you believe how silly this is, clearly manipulated." 

>Like holy shit. Zero possibility in their mind that they are wrong, haven't cut any losses, haven't sold a single share of stock...ask an LLM about this, I can't, I literally can't.

>this is not a "disconnected" tape, nor is it a "factor" tape, the market is razor fucking sharp right now on separating winners vs losers, repricing them in in real-time as incremental newsflow arrives, and reallocating mkt cap accordingly. 

>I don't even like commenting on current moves like this, and I never wish red P&L on anyone (horrible karma), its just hard watching new ppl learn old lessons, and confirmation bias puts a man in what is really a jaw-dropping state of delusion

>not semis (they've been riding a multi-yr winning trend) but all rest of tech...if u didn't sell a share down 10%, 20%, 30% or 40%, and now ur at 50-60% and still refuse to believe u may be missing something? Lord...

>general rule, when the index is printing record highs and your stock is down some 20%, first instinct should be stop out --> ask "am I missing something" --> reunderwrite from scratch. you can always buy it back. you rarely will.

Just listen to the tape, that's the point of all this, at least entertain what it may be telling u...down 50% with the index @ record highs is a position u should never end up in.

arcana
>with all of this, always ask "how can this practice help me as fundamental analyst" + how can it be part of my process, etc

>avoid going too deep into technical-analysis land, this isn't meant to be that. this is additive to a fundamental analyst process

>no L/S idea gen should ever have shit like stochastic oscillators or MACD or moving averages in it

>everything is two-sided in markets - if a stock is ramping higher, one man sees a mean-reversion trade, the other sees a momentum trade, and both could be right. thats why u keep this shit out of ur idea gen

>at the end of the day this is all sort of "arcana." The no-mans land, the DMZ, between fundamentals/technicals...ur looking for unusual px action that could signal an inflection in fundamentals.

--

Overall: FORM YOUR OWN OPINION. In fundamentals, go right to the source docs, and in reading tape, go right to the charts. 

The LLM post is below bc this is all another part of one's education, so just plug everything written above into that framework.

I'll link a few other posts below this on fundamentals/technicals + grinding charts.

good luck trading, be humble, listen to the tape, and USA (Use Stops Always).

> QT @gregoryblotnick: Learning Markets, Modeling & How LLM's Reduce "Time-To-Mastery" by 50-90%

## 238. 2026-07-17
<a id="t-2078065605047894473"></a>

*Single post · [2078065605047894473](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2078065605047894473)*

on organizing info/ideas -- both reading & writing

in the QT it walks thru a process for paperback readers

the next step is developing a system for writing, and keeping track of all your ideas so u can build them off one another

this is a work in progress but so far I'm content with writing either articles, or longer tweets/threads and then periodically compiling them into an article (every 3-6 months, or at year-end)

this ensures that your writing doesn't just disappear into the ephemerality of twtr. also bc search function never works

if you are trying to seriously improve as a writer, u need the 6-12 mo old material to reflect on

if it makes u cringe then thats good. it means u improved

if u dont have any 6-12 month old shit, or it doesnt make u cringe, ur not writing enough

its nice having all ur tweets/articles handy because I'm always trying to build towers higher and connect topics that arent related at all

so once you have a nice base of writing built out, it makes it real easy to go grab something, QT it, then add links in it ("also X here and Y here"), plus a reply tweet ("for more, heres an article") and the reader can follow along

is that good for engagement, honestly I dont give a shit, this is free and not for $....for engagement my guess is that 280 character tweets would be better, but thats not my LT goal, I'm writing to write long-form and books

ultimately I like sharing interesting stuff and giving a reader 2-3 "if u liked this, go here next" paths

reason being, because if I enjoyed YOUR writing, that's exactly what I'd want from you -- send me down a rabbit hole man, give me the good shit, and make it as frictionless as possible so I can keep poking around.

"is there a better platform for doing this"...man who gives a fuck lol

if ur writing is good then ppl will read it, and if its trash then no one will care...its just as with anything else in life, where every platform is what u make of it.

hth / more to come

> QT @gregoryblotnick: a crude but functional system for paperback readers

>take iphone photos of pages/sections you enjoyed

>periodically select a bunch of them, “copy iCloud link,” email to self

>on desktop, download all the photos

>send all the photos to LLM and say “transcribe these to text for me”

>save all the text to a Word doc, annotate/organize/add headers as u see fit

>wait 2-3 mo and tweet what “sticks”

twtr can be used for many things but my #1 has always been “book excerpt pseudo-cloud-storage, with an added kicker that maybe someone else reads it and it helps them think differently.”

so even if u don’t poast…u can still get a “Paperback Highlight Reel” out of this hellscape app. 📚

## 240. 2026-07-17
<a id="t-2078091540564410681"></a>

*Single post · [2078091540564410681](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2078091540564410681)*

the hardest part about adjusting ur way of operating to Buffett's credo -- “If I do it for you, I have to do it for everyone” - means saying no to people all the time

just never been the way I was wired, I've had to cremate old versions of myself and start fresh

for 30+ years I don't think I said the word "no" once...simply wasn't in my vocabulary

every spontaneous choice or party or drug or impulsive decision = "yes" without thinking

this is not a sustainable way to go thru life for many reasons and reflects someone w/ no core and no principles

"people pleaser" is not a good thing fyi, its far more vice than virtue, the roots of that tree are rotten

meditate on that if u dont get it...LLMs are actually good for this type of shit

ask them about "addictive personality" while you're there -- they'll confirm that its not a thing, doesn't exist, bullshit term used by ppl with no self-discipline as a crutch.

but as u switch to "no as a default answer" - this means disappointing people and irking their delicate sensibilities.

no one likes being told no. no one ever will.

but in exchange...u will begrudgingly gain the respect of everyone and anyone u deal with, whether their bruised ego openly admits it or not.

I can explain further on this if u want, having been at both extremes, and understanding the path u walk to get here. because its counterintuitive. u feel as if u are walking away from selfless and towards selfish.

but read abt self-betrayal, about setting boundaries, and about how "you can't pour from an empty cup"...rest should become clear.

> QT @gregoryblotnick: one other quote in here from Buffett that I think a lot about

"If I do it for you, I have to do it for everyone."

its something that many of u have prob arrived at independently...or at least, I have

comes back to same core principles of, u gotta treat everyone the exact same, set boundaries, communicate where the "line" is, and don't waver if and when that line is crossed

bc the minute u bend for one person, its over...everyone sees that ur principles are mutable. a boundary loses all power once it proves to be subjective, rather than objective...

## 241. 2026-07-17
<a id="t-2078102823502327842"></a>

*Single post · [2078102823502327842](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2078102823502327842)*

Writing = "a vivid and continuous fictional dream."

this is another serious book -- John Gardner really is the "axe" -- but one which can be enjoyed as a reader of novels/fiction, anyone who appreciates art, or the craft, or is a student of the game, or who just wants to know more about what makes great writing great

that term ("vivid and continuous dream") is all I want to highlight in this post, I won't summarize the whole book, nor could I tbh, as half of it went over my head

Gardner will just casually pick apart in random paragraphs the faults of a Faulkner or Hemingway, and not as some loser critic, but just as someone with a master eye for the craft... he senses details that you or I wouldn't catch in 1000 years

but thinking of a great piece of writing as "a vivid dream" is such an astute term...novel readers want to be so caught up in the "dream" that they forget they're even reading.

and so good reading and good writing, are both a function of remaining that the vivid and continuous dream remains unbroken...which is prob why encountering an LLM generated sentence feels mentally like crashing into a 80-car pileup

I pulled together 10 quotes from him, from reviewers, and from around the internet to drill this point home:

--

1. “All good books are alike in that they are truer than if they had really happened and after you are finished reading one you will feel that all that happened to you and afterwards it belongs to you; the good and the bad, the ecstasy, the remorse and sorrow, the people and the places and how the weather was.”

2. ...By continuous, he means nothing on the page distracts the reader from the story, nothing makes him backtrack, nothing makes him have to pause and think -- he may pause and think because something stirs his imagination, but we don't want him to pause because he has to figure something out to understand the story.

3. "Writer and teacher John Gardner had a concept he called the fictional dream, which was the idea that fiction does its job by creating a dream state for the reader, and as long as the writer is doing a good job of maintaining that dream state, the reader won't "wake up" from it and will continue to read and believe in the fictional world the writer has created. 

4. Gardner argues that this fictional dream first happens in the writer's head, and the writer's job is to write it down for the reader...Gardner's central argument holds that effective fiction must create a "vivid and continuous dream" in the reader's mind, an immersive experience that engages the reader emotionally and intellectually as if the events were real. This requires the writer to uphold moral and aesthetic seriousness, treating fiction as a rigorous art form committed to truth, precise detail, and human values rather than mere entertainment or self-expression."

5. Central to his framework is the notion that great fiction creates a "vivid and continuous fictional dream" in which the reader experiences the narrative world as immediate and real, engaging the senses and emotions in a manner akin to lived experience.

6. Gardner describes this dream as arising when the writer presents dramatized actions through concrete, sensory images rather than abstract exposition, allowing readers to perceive settings, characters, and events with full conviction: "the writer sets up a dramatized action in which we are given the signals that make us 'see' the setting, characters and events... so that we seem to move among the characters, lean with them against the fictional walls, taste the fictional gazpacho, smell the fictional hyacinths."

7. He stresses that anything interrupting this immersion—such as technical errors, authorial intrusions, or deliberate reminders of the text's constructed nature—snaps the reader out of the experience, rendering the fiction unsatisfying. Great fiction, in contrast, sustains the illusion so powerfully that emotional responses mirror those in real life: "Great fiction can make us laugh or cry, in much the way that life can... Somehow the fictional dream persuades us that it’s a clear, sharp, edited version of the dream all around us."

8. Gardner "...emphasizes the creation of a "vivid and continuous fictional dream" for the reader, treating any disruption—whether from clumsy prose or emotional inauthenticity—as a serious flaw."

9. Gardner presents the "vivid and continuous dream" as the central metaphor for successful fiction, asserting that good writing must immerse the reader in an uninterrupted, convincing fictional world that feels real and absorbing. The "vivid" element requires precise, concrete details, strong verbs, fresh imagery, and emotional authenticity to generate clear mental pictures and powerful feelings, while the "continuous" aspect depends on flawless execution to prevent any disruption—such as factual errors, inconsistencies, awkward shifts in perspective, or clumsy phrasing—from breaking the reader's immersion. Gardner stresses that any flaw waking the reader from this dream state undermines the fiction, making the creation of this seamless experience the writer's primary obligation.

10. One of the book's most lasting contributions is its popularization of the concept of the "vivid and continuous dream," which holds that successful fiction must create an immersive, uninterrupted experience in the reader's mind, appealing to the senses and sustaining narrative logic without drawing attention to the text itself. This principle has become a central tenet in teaching realist fiction, guiding writers to avoid disruptions such as poor diction or self-conscious language that might jolt the reader from immersion. Gardner's discussions also helped bring terms like "psychic distance"—the varying degrees of emotional and perceptual closeness between narrator and character—and "profluence"—the forward causal momentum that propels a story—into common usage among writers and educators.

--

last, from the writer's perspective:

“In the writing state—the state of inspiration—the fictive dream springs up fully alive: the writer forgets the words he has written on the page and sees, instead, his characters moving around their rooms, hunting through cupboards, glancing irritably through their mail, setting mousetraps, loading pistols. The dream is as alive and compelling as one’s dreams at night, and when the writer writes down on paper what he has imagined, the words, however inadequate, do not distract his mind from the fictive dream but provide him with a fix on it, so that when the dream flags he can reread what he’s written and find the dream starting up again. This and nothing else is the desperately sought and tragically fragile writer’s process: in his imagination, he sees made-up people doing things—sees them clearly—and in the act of wondering what they will do next he sees what they will do next, and all this he writes down in the best, most accurate words he can find, understanding even as he writes that he may have to find better words later, and that a change in the words may mean a sharpening or deepening of the vision, the fictive dream or vision becoming more and more lucid, until reality, by comparison, seems cold, tedious, and dead.”

--

if ur a serious reader of novels both books are worth your time ("art of fiction" first, "becoming a novelist" second)...and it should go without saying that if you have even the faintest interest in writing well, both are a drop-what-you're-doing and buy them immediately.

dream on
GB

> QT @gregoryblotnick: really good book on writing, I think one of the canonical texts

even if ur just a reader - "writing makes you a better reader, and reading makes you a better writer" - this book fits that

(1/4)

"There are only two plots in literature: a hero goes on a journey, or a stranger comes to town." -- that quote gets attributed to Gardner and is why I bought the book

turns out its apocryphal. but theres a lot in here thats just as valuable, and its easily something he could have said

its very clear this man is a master and is fluent not just in the craft but the way he dissects everyone from Faulkner to Dostoevsky, in language that did not occur to u naturally

this is not a "difficult" book re: length or language used. however, its very challenging in that a single sentence will make you stop and reflect on how applicable it is to you. you get out of it what you put into it

(2/4)

people see "creative writing" and think, I dont write, I dont need that. this is wrong

much of creative writing/fiction is simply your ability to describe day-to-day life or human nature

examples
-describe the face of the woman you love
-describe the way your friend walks
-an old hemingway one: watch people getting out of taxis and describe how they differ

etc...once u try this, and put words on paper, u will realize that ur writing is horrible, nondescript or cliche

then u will look at ur bookshelf and say "I need to see how the old masters did this"

then u realize u never really "read" these books.

this is how writing makes u a better reader.

(3/4)

whether an ability to describe everyday life is important to u or not...idk

if ur wired like me, as soon as a weakness of yours is brought to ur attention, u will not rest until its removed or progress is made

if ur reading this u prob come from a business background where u are instructed, more or less, to "tell, dont show"

this is the opposite of writing about life, where the cardinal rule is 

"Show, don't tell."

so u have to both unlearn some of what u know, plus add an entirely different skillset.

(4/4)

I finished this 3-4 weeks ago and it has been bouncing around in my head since...thats how u know it was good

I never really rec shit that I just finished, bc it takes time to see if the writing had staying power.  the great ones plant themselves in your subconscious

so I would say if you are someone who either appreciates great writing, or wants to improve for their own sake, this should prob be on ur shelf, and is one u will end up reading multiple times.

## 242. 2026-07-17
<a id="t-2078110947030221193"></a>

*Single post · [2078110947030221193](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2078110947030221193)*

comedic and somewhat unique aspect of markets to never lose sight of

its the only game where u can try as hard as possible to lose, and end up doing better than u are right now.

in practice, if u sat down, blank sheet of paper, and said “I’m going to build the absolute worst portfolio imaginable”

there’s a pretty good chance it would outperform ur current one

this is probably worth doing as a thought exercise far more often than people actually do it

bc for most people, chances are that it'll illustrate the "worst" ideas, then you'll go to the opposite of that, it'll be what you own, which is what everyone else owns, which means it trades flat thru 2030

even applies across asset classes...u might think "I'll sell puts on Costco with a $10 strike, thats my best idea"

but if someone's making markets in it, no idea is bad. fast forward 6 years, the mkt corrects and the puts 100x and u lose.

anyway, this = 52w lows over $2B mkt cap...strange tape where the "worst possible portfolio" may be a bunch of companies with no revenue.

## 243. 2026-07-17
<a id="t-2078125409573511633"></a>

*Single post · [2078125409573511633](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2078125409573511633)*

all change is driven by contempt and disgust for some part of urself. the term I've settled on is "a healthy self-loathing"

bordering almost on hatred, some shame and some cringe, but more of a fierce intolerance for mediocrity and weakness within urself

that visceral level of disgust is what creates a sense of urgency where u just cant continue another day in ur current state. it would be unbearable

this is why all change comes from within and why u cant change other people, it has to have that powerful internal drive for it to be sustainable

really, this should be ongoing

if u arent sick to ur stomach when u look back on who u used to be 2-3 years ago, u havent grown 

this is the only way u improve at markets and risk as far as I know. u have to hate urself for making such dumb mistakes that they sear themselves into ur gut instinct and are never repeated again

the flipside is to avoid veering into a depressive spiral or negative + self-destructive feedback loops.

key is to reject BEHAVIORS and not reject urself

a part of u is worthless, a vice, that needs to be erased and jettisoned

but if u enter "I'm worthless," u can destroy ur self confidence to the point where stagnancy or negative momentum kick in.

a lot depends on ur personality type, u know which way u err...people either arent hard enough on themselves (no improvement) or too hard (negative spiral)

in both instances u gotta just remember that a man's life comes in seasons

u can leave any/all problematic behaviors behind

no one gives a shit, its always 100% u against urself or some story u tell urself about why ur a certain way, but its meaningless -- a "load bearing delusion"

and so once u have this all calibrated correctly -- the self-loathing and contempt, in the healthiest possible sense of course -- u can pick up momentum very quickly

especially after 3-6 months of execution when u can actually see results, changes in behavior, and u realize, like...oh shit

a lot more of this is in my control than I thought

boom, thats when its really on

and now u understand the meaning of the quote:

"man cannot remake himself without suffering... for he is both the marble and the sculptor."

## 247. 2026-07-18
<a id="t-2078297761867341913"></a>

*Single post · [2078297761867341913](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2078297761867341913)*

this message is important, but also nuanced around age (20-40):

"NO ONE CARES HOW HARD YOU WORK."

but u really want to understand this, at all levels, all ages...because like a lot of human nature, its part fact and also part horseshit. 

example one, the idea of "face time." 

1/5

should it matter for ur ass to be in that seat, should it matter that u are "seen" busy and working...no, it shouldnt. ur work should matter.

but DOES it matter?

hell yeah, especially at first

if shit goes wrong, one guy has been "ass in seat," and the other guy has been "working from Jitney" on Fridays...one guy is relatively safe.

or when ppl talk about the first kid, "don't really know him, he works hard tho, he's always here early and stays late"

thats gonna matter. so at first, yes, people do care how hard u work, or more accurately, that u give off the appearance of working.

this is the same "sorta bullshit" bucket as all degrees, certifications, pedigree, CV stamps, et cetera...its 90% bullshit, but it matters.

2/5

NOW...as u move up the layer cake, face time matters less and less, and performance matters more and more. 

at least, it should, or ur career path doesn't have much by way of motivation and upside.

but let's say u turn 30, you've done everything right, and u bounce to go try and build something.

at that moment...you fully enter the "NO ONE CARES HOW HARD YOU WORK" zone.

you have to immediately unlearn and incinerate your 10-20 years of accumulated corporate indoctrination

all that straight up bullshit, politics, face time, etc

on your own, NONE of that shit is relevant.

you either perform or you don't..."losers whine about their best, winners go home and f*** the prom queen," as Sean Connery put it.

3/5

you have partners, investors, employees, an orbit of ppl linked to ur success

for a lot of businesses, that comes down to a quarterly report with a few numbers in it:

"we grew 5%," "we returned 8%," "we lost 1%"

that's it. that number is your job, all these people in your orbit are there for that output

absolutely no one gives a fuck how hard you work.

if u work ur ass off and its a (10%), "maybe dont work so hard next time"

if u spend all month on the beach, do 1 hour of work, and its a +20%... u should do that.

this message, I feel like is not pounded into people's heads enough

especially because u are full of habits from age 20 to 30, that now need to be destroyed as fast as possible, unlearned, ripped out.

investment mgmt can be comical in that your effort and your results, at times, just have zero correlation

we want to believe they do, but the truth is that completely random shit goes down a lot, and it either lifts u or smokes u

4/5

so...in addition to my message of "no one cares how hard u work," there is a LONG journey of self-exploration as u re-optimize ur life

u must keep rebuilding fresh, asking: "how should I spend my days that maximizes my performance?"

this is why I spend so much time talking and thinking about habits and attitude...work matters but the ROI on habits is much much higher

I don't see/hear enough people talking or thinking this way, which is why I'm being forceful on the message.

good habits can bail out bad decisions, but bad habits cant save good decisions.

I might catch a stray for being super results-focused on all this, but u gotta understand, no one ever writes u a check or sends a wire because they think ur a fun guy

Motherfuckers want to get paid

5/5

These are not friendships, don't ever confuse the two...u can test it out firsthand, send a dummy/mistake email that says "we lost 80% yesterday from daytrading crypto" and see how people react

Some people are going to be heated as hell

So take this shit seriously, and question anyone who is like "oh you're too focused on that quarterly performance estimate" or whatever

Out of 100 people, maybe 1 or 2 are dialed in and hyper-competitive, the other 98 are just tossing random comments onto the field. On this topic, find older versions of the 1-2, and listen to them, because they have experience -- the rest has zero.

What do u think people sign an LP agreement for? There IS no other variable, its business. The worst people, the much-maligned types, are dudes who suck ass at their job, but write tons of blogs and letters and do podcasts instead. 

Every LP is thinking "maybe this jackass should do his job instead of blathering about AGI"

Results matter, outcomes matter, proof of concept matters, execution matters, doing exactly what you said you were going to do matters

You do that long enough and maybe u can open ur mouth here and there

But realistically, its in everyones best interest - including your own - to just spend the first 3-5 years doing two things: STFU, and execute.

END

Until u have proven u can execute, there is nothing to talk about.

You worked really long hours last year, but you couldn't execute? 

No one cares. In fact, if u don't execute this year, that's two in a row and most people will (rightfully) redeem, take their ball and go home, and try to find someone who isn't a fucking loser, someone who can execute worth a shit.

Again, if I sound too hard nosed, or this sounds unpleasant, go stay at some giant cushy office job...they are designed around redundancies, mediocrity, and to not miss a beat if u died tomorrow. For some people this sounds great, to others it sounds like prison.

If u want to go do ur own thing, u are the business. So all tolerance for bullshit has to die an immediate death.

I think this principle has been made clear, and ur mind can frame it around 20, 30, 40, or wherever you're at

But the most important message in here, by far, is to recognize when u have entered the "100% results" stage

Because right there, nothing else matters...no one cares how hard you work. Destroy and rebuild ur life from scratch and start the lifelong process of reaching "peak performance" for lack of a better word.

And remember, the stakes are high, but so is the upside...if u come out those first 3-5 years and knock the goddamn cover off the ball, everyone will come in for more, send referrals, and help u with marketing/raising.

The dream has always been there and always will be. There's just a very very small subset of mfs who take risk and bet on themselves to begin with, and then within that subset, 99% will fail.

So if you're that 1%, and you can execute, like, the entire damn world is yours. Straight facts.

Never forget: "Pressure is a privilege." 

So treat it like one...

## 253. 2026-07-18
<a id="t-2078436289221689474"></a>

*Single post · [2078436289221689474](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2078436289221689474)*

5 Druck highlights

1. Sizing: "What I learned from Soros is that sizing is 70% to 80% of the equation."

2. Portfolio Construction: "If you want to survive in this business for multiple decades, you have to look at your portfolio every single morning as a completely blank slate. It doesn’t matter what price you bought a stock at, and it doesn't matter what your thesis was yesterday. If the facts change, or if the price action tells you the market is rejecting your thesis, you have to swallow your pride and get out."

3. Micro First: "I would tell [22-year olds] to focus entirely on learning how to analyze industries from the bottom up, rather than trying to become a pure macro forecaster right out of the gate. Like I mentioned, I started as a chemical and banking analyst. You need to understand how businesses actually make money, how supply chains operate, and how competitive advantages are built or destroyed before you can aggregate that into a global economic view."

4. Be Yourself/Find Your Own Style: "And finally, you have to find your own style. You can't just copy Stan Druckenmiller, or Warren Buffett, or John Collison. If you try to trade or invest using someone else’s framework, you won’t have the conviction to stick with it when a trade goes against you. You have to build a toolkit that matches your own psychological risk tolerance."

5. On Passion/Work Ethic: "I was pretty lazy in college and I never considered myself a particularly hard worker, but I'm so passionate about our business that it's almost like a compulsive gambler who has found a way to channel his compulsion."

"I've seen young people who kind of looked like they were lazy, had lost their way, or weren't very driven, but when they find their passion, they become very driven. I just happen to be passionate about this particular discipline. So, I don't know whether I naturally have a hard work ethic, but that is the end result of my passion."

--

## 264. 2026-07-18
<a id="t-2078526657086787913"></a>

*Single post · [2078526657086787913](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2078526657086787913)*

List of banks/financial institutions who wanted Buffett as a backstop in 2008

All got denied except GS and GE

Denied Bear, denied Lehman three separate times, denied Barclays, Freddie Mac, and AIG.

Insane to me that he could even assess the risk of some black-box bank in a 2008 backdrop, or what pushed GS over the edge aside from terms + personal relationships

Color on how he thinks / Why Lehman Got Denied

March 28, 2008 – Lehman Brothers (First direct call)
Lehman CEO Dick Fuld personally called Buffett. He asked if Berkshire would invest at least $2 billion in Lehman.

Buffett was immediately concerned about two things:
Lehman executives were apparently unwilling to invest significantly on the same terms.
Fuld kept complaining about short sellers instead of acknowledging problems at Lehman.

After the call, Buffett reviewed Lehman’s latest 10-K filing and discovered a ~$100 million undisclosed problem in Japan that Fuld had not mentioned.

→ Buffett quickly decided against investing. He later said they “never got close to a deal.”

--

the rest, like AIG -- too deep and too uncertain of a hole...Barclays wanted more than he was willing to provide, the rest, seems like it was on terms

In all of these, he reaches a decision shockingly fast...often same day

Calculator for a brain, shark, ice cold bond trader, that's who he is, prob the sharpest mind for weighing and assessing risk that we will ever see.

## 266. 2026-07-18
<a id="t-2078537427652932055"></a>

*Single post · [2078537427652932055](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2078537427652932055)*

Partnership-era Buffett (1956-1969)

brief thoughts + summary

(link to letters: https://t.co/5kfIeN4r3V)

1. Style

cigar butts and undervalued asset plays

We covered Sanborn and Dempster (control situations)

Amex in 1964-1966, that was a 40% position, after the Salad Oil scandal

Frequently took 20-30% positions, usually had top 2 or 3 comprising 40%

Other notable holdings: British Columbia Power, Cleveland Worsted Mills, Greif Bros, Commonwealth Trust Co, smaller positions in Disney and Berkshire Hathaway

No quality, no LT/tax driven style, just Generals, Workouts and Controls -- situations that are cheap, have upside levers to create value, or where I can become the majority holder.

Partnership compounded at 31% gross / 25% net from 1957-1969

2.

So no matter who he became or what narratives get put on him, you gotta remember that at his core, he is wired for cap structure arbitrage, special sits, he just sees an enterprise and hits the hole like an RB. 

Here are the 3-4 ways value can be created for me here, even if its zero sum. 

Very wide/diverse weaponry and toolkit, I literally don't understand a lot of the shit he does in these names...moving around debt and equity and tax assets and cash and balance sheet items and blowing out the board and monetizing some investment held for sale or real estate and then boom, somehow 25% return pops out.

Most specialists people have one skill in there, I have zero, he somehow has all of them.

I don't go too deep into any of the investments in here just because I know this is never an arena or style where I have a shot...dummy at the table, I would get waxed by event-driven specialists. 

3.

Also the style itself has gotten arbed out/less inefficient, information edge was real back then, much more room to just go start punching motherfuckers in the face to affect change

Is there anything else in these letters, are they worth your time...not unless you've read all the modern-day BRK ones, along with everything Buffett has ever written. These are lower-tier Buffett (which is still better than 99% of other shit). Just not a lot that can directly improve you.

4.

But for the cultist/fanatic, they are great, esp because u see he was just rocking and rolling, cocky and confident, executing, putting motherfuckers brains all over the wall, and talking the perfect amount of shit relative to it all. 

You want to see that, u know he was never just folksy old dickhead. That hunger, thats always present in the younger versions of these dudes. Fierce fierce competitors, always, you can just tell that you'd lose 100% of these fights.

Also just the raw passion, start out a letter "We bought Sanborn Map" and then dump 3 pages out about this shitco as if any LP really understands what the hell is going on

Its like 1 sentence "they make maps" and 3 pages "here's the cap structure and here's what I'm gonna do to it," brutally boring at times but he calls his shots and he absolutely loves cigar butts, thats just what the game was back then....schooled by Ben Graham

He is clearly obsessive about taxes already, rambles about it, he just never had the vehicles here to really crank up the deferral/float machine.

END

That's about all I've got, you can prob dump all this into an LLM and ask "whats he missing" for more.

## 267. 2026-07-18
<a id="t-2078565110214443091"></a>

*Single post · [2078565110214443091](https://x.com/gregoryblotnick/status/2078565110214443091)*

random but if ur in a drawdown rn, say 25-50%, which I sense many are because everyone is in the same trades

“When you’re in a hole, stop digging”

It tends to be a net benefit to eat a bunch of losses, clean up the P&L screen, and refresh ur mind. Unrealized P&L clogs the brain, always

Rethink all the losing positions that u exited, start from stage zero 

There is nothing stopping u from putting the same trade right back on if u decide its best - there’s wash sales to account for, that’s it

But u cannot underestimate mental capital, and the simple “clear the decks” of having an emptier book + capital to deploy

Turns u opportunistic again vs defensive and anxious 

“You don’t have to make it back the way you lost it”

The biggest risk is that many inexperienced ppl go on tilt when they’re down say 50%, “I’m already so fucked”

Blowing out of losses puts in the bottom for ur near term P&L. It’s always more refreshing than it is negative 

The other mental pitfall is ppl assuming that the stock has to go here or there, or back where it came from

Brotha - it doesn’t have to do ANYTHING. The current price is the price. Nothing is guaranteed beyond that

And so again, eating L’s cuts off all those wirings in your brain, refreshes anchoring and cost basis. No more “should”

Next, u have ppl saying “what if I miss the rebound”

Bro if u are trading on that short a time frame, 1-2 weeks, even less than 1 month, u just aren’t approaching this thoughtfully…very herky jerky, day to day, driven by FOMO and non fundamental factors

You must eliminate “what if I miss a 2 week rally right after I sell it” from your brain

This is pure bad. All bad. Will only lead u astray. 

Until you’re comfortable just chilling and watching, and watching a stock rally after u sell it without freaking or chasing…ur emotions aren’t where they need to be. 

You visualize, u say “I am taking the L, I am ready for it to go up 30% right after I click sell, and that’s just fuckin life. There’s 100000 other stocks. I’ll find another”

Could write endlessly on this but being in a big draw over a weekend, v easy to be fucked mentally, sweating futures open, like you are driven by fear and emotion and HOPE. None of which are viable 

Your best bet is to blow the fuck out of anything down big causing u agita. You can buy it back higher in a month if it was a bad sale. But think process not outcome. Also given that ur trading like shit, taking a week off to heal the mind, keeping core longs on, is only gonna help u

Brings u back with fresh eyes

So Mon ideally, u go from 100% invested and in 50% drawdown, to just being mostly cash. Start from scratch. Ur capital base is ur capital base, the stock prices are the stock prices. History is HISTORY - it’s IRRELEVANT. 

Protect ur mental capital

I’ve made every mistake and watched 500 ppl make the same ones so I tend to know the ruts where ppl are at…I feel it from DM’s

Down 30-50%, like, fuck, I liked this idea but I’m smoked and the tape sucks

Take the L, accept that u may miss a sharp rebound, LIVE WITH IT. 

There is a hard ceiling for any trader who cannot take L’s with equanimity

You will never progress until u are cool as a fan with lock-in of losses, mental refresh, stabilize, then go on attack.

U can attack in an entirely new sector or name, like swap into what’s printing ATH’s, stop fighting secular bear overhangs, and chances are u do not belong in semis

Rethink it all

“Stop doing what isn’t working”

Mental capital is everything. Stay liquid and nimble and opportunistic. Deep red unrealized losses are poisonous roots that disrupt clear thinking

Cut those motherfuckers on sight. All temporary. All can be re entered later. 

Protect ur mental kiddo. Good luck trading
